The answers of all three carry `mirror` = `{state, key, note}`: a page that could not be written (`failed`, e.g. the page store refused the put twice, or the page lists an agent the record lacks) does not fail the call, since the record already holds the change and the project's next change writes the page again. Optional: without the section these are the defaults. Contract: [21, the web terminal's project calls](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md). | |
| `project_agent.form_machines` | `[]` | The machines whose token may make a form call (see `form_senders`), e.g. `["myhost"]`; empty = no call is the form's. | |
| `steps.owner_shell` | `""` | Where the owner runs the commands of a board step. `"powershell"` = in PowerShell on a Windows machine: a command in a `next` line that starts "&lt;owner&gt;:" must say where it runs ("In PowerShell: ..."), and a command for the Linux host is written `ssh -t <bash_host> '<command>'`. `"sala-shell"` = in sala's Shell, a terminal in the sala web UI that runs bash on `bash_host` (paste there with Shift+Insert, not Ctrl+V): a command is bash as typed there, or `ssh <host> '<command>'` for another machine; no line saying where it runs is asked for, and a PowerShell or Windows-only command (a cmdlet, `winget`, a drive path) outside ssh is refused. Empty = no place is asked for: a command only has to be in backticks. | yes |
| `steps.bash_host` | `""` | The Linux host such a bash command is run on over ssh (`powershell`), or the host sala's Shell runs on (`sala-shell`); empty = none named. | yes |
| `steps.shell_name` | `""` | `sala-shell` only: the name of the owner's Shell session in sala, written into the tool text beside "sala's Shell"; empty = none named. | yes |
| `terminal.fallback_project` | `""` | The project page a terminal-prompt item is filed under when the session's own project lists no front agent; empty = none (the item is refused with that reason). | |
| `runners.repos` | `[]` | The repos whose self-hosted runners are read. Empty (or no `[runners]` table) = the board does not use runners: the page and the side panel's Runners section say so in one plain line and nothing is read. | |
| `runners.owner` | `""` | The GitHub owner (login) of the repos in `runners.repos`, for a repo with no ID under `[runners.ids]`; empty = none set (such a repo reads "set [runners] owner"). | |
| `runners.boxes` | `[]` | The machine rows of the CI runners page, e.g. `["myhost"]`. Rows follow the Machines panel's order (the Runners page and the side panel's Runners section list a machine where the Machines panel does); a machine the Machines panel does not list goes after those, in this order, then the other machines the labels named. | |
| `runners.local_box` | `""` | The box this server runs on: its CI switch is read from local files; empty = none (every box then reads "switch not read"). | |
| `runners.switch_script` | `""` | The switch script the CI runners page's Disable / Enable button runs, as `bash <switch_script> off\|on <box>` on this server (`~` allowed); it ssh's to the other machines itself. Empty = no buttons, and `POST /runners/switch` refuses. | |
| `runners.switch_timeout_s` | `90` | One button press is given this long; past it the script and what it started are stopped and the page says so. | |
| `runners.ssh_hosts.<box>` | none | The ssh host that reaches the box's CI switch; no entry = a fleet machine is reached as `ssh <name>`, any other box is not read over ssh. Every machine's switch is read this way (in parallel), for the page's state line and its button. A box that `runners.boxes` lists and this table names (a machine outside the sala fleet, such as spare-box) gets the Disable / Enable button too, which a box with no entry here and no fleet membership never does; the switch script reaches it as `ssh <box>`, so the host written here should be the box's own name. | |
| `model.migration_senders` | `[]` | The sessions that may run the item model migration pass; empty = the pass is refused until they are named. | |
| `model.migration_machines` | `[]` | The machines whose token may; empty = none. | |
| `model.rebullet_machines` | `[]` | The machines whose token may write through `/client/rebullet`; empty = the call is refused. | |
| `said.injected_prefixes` | `[]` | A message starting with one of these is text a tool or a brief typed into the pane, not the owner's words (e.g. `["[auto-note]", "Relay note:"]`). | |
| `said.injected_phrases` | `[]` | The same, for a phrase anywhere in the message. | |
| `machines_panel.latest_lookups` | `true` | false never asks npm or GitHub for the latest versions and keeps the cache as it is. | |
| `machines_panel.packages[].name` | `"claude code"` | One row of the Machines panel, top to bottom. | |
| `machines_panel.packages[].owner` | `""` | The session with jurisdiction over the package; the card links to it. | |
| `machines_panel.packages[].installed` | `"fleet"` | fleet (per machine, from its reported CLI version) or `cmd:<command>` (this box). | |
| `machines_panel.packages[].self_cmd` | `"~/.local/bin/claude --version"` | How the server box reads its own copy. | if used |
| `machines_panel.packages[].latest` | `"npm:@anthropic-ai/claude-code"` | Where the latest version comes from: `npm:<package>` or `github-release:<owner/repo>`. | |
| `gbrain.mirror_url` | `"https://github.com/example/brain/blob/main/{key}.md"` | The link the board puts on a page key. | yes |
| `gbrain.bin` | `"gbrain"` | The gbrain command line, a name on PATH or a path (`~` allowed): only a `[pages] flavor = "gbrain"` instance uses it (`rename_page.py`, `page_keeper.py`, the router's timeline notes). | |
| `gbrain.home` | unset | `GBRAIN_HOME` for the default brain's gbrain CLI calls; unset = the CLI's own default home (its environment is left as it is). | |
| `[[brains]]` | none | Second notes brains that hold some projects' pages (`brains.py`): `name`, `path` (its local git copy, read only for stackshot), `home` (`GBRAIN_HOME` for its writes), `secret_file` (optional: a file holding the CLI's OAuth client secret, passed to that brain's CLI calls only; see [Second brains](#second-brains)), `mirror_url` (page links, `{key}`), `slugs` and/or `prefixes` (the page keys it holds; sub-pages follow their page), `new_prefixes` and `keep` (where a page that exists in no brain yet starts; see [Second brains](#second-brains)), `stale_hours` (default 24; 0 = never stale). Every page read (the registry, create's page check, the page hook, pages owed, the audit's page call, page links) resolves the slug to its brain and reads that brain's copy; every page write runs the gbrain CLI with that brain's home. Fail open: when its copy is missing or stale, or its CLI call fails, create's page check and the page hook accept the item and log a warning naming the brain, pages owed and the audit's page call skip its pages for the run (the page call tries again at the next audit run when the CLI read failed) and say so once per outage in each process that reads (the state is per brain; one more line says the brain is back when its reads work again), and the registry keeps its pages as last read; a copy that exists but is no usable copy (no `.git`, or no page in it: an unmounted folder, a clone in progress) counts as missing; an entry with no `home` is not reached through the CLI: a read or a write for its slugs is refused. With none, every page is in the default brain, as before. See [Second brains](#second-brains). | |
| `anchor_hub.enabled` | `false` | true: `install.sh` installs and enables anchor-hub's user unit (the receivers need their tokens in `<secrets>/anchor-hub/env`); false: the unit is not installed, and an existing one is left alone. | if used |
| `anchor_hub.hook_url` | `"https://hooks.example.com"` | The public address of the webhook receiver (Drive, Gmail, Notion push). | if used |
| `anchor_hub.hook_port` | `8010` | The local port of the webhook receiver. | |
| `anchor_hub.self_emails` | `[]` | The owner's own addresses: their Notion edits and comments are not news. | if used |
| `anchor_hub.slack_team` | `""` | The Slack permalink host when `auth_test` carries no URL. | |
| `anchor_hub.slack_allow_self_channels` | `[]` | Slack channel ids where the owner's own posts and edits are delivered like anyone's (a bot's post stays dropped); elsewhere the hub's own user is not news. A list, or one comma- or whitespace-separated string. | |
| `anchor_hub.direct` | `{}` | The direct lane: anchor id to one session, e.g. `{ "slack:C0123ABCD" = "my-session" }`. Each event on that anchor goes at once to that session alone, one notice per event, with the whole text (clipped at 4,000 chars) and, for Slack, its `ts` and `thread_ts`; the project bindings, the pilot, mention filters and the debounce do not apply, and the notice is logged with target `direct` and nothing to rule on. Every bot's Slack post (`bot_id`, or subtype `bot_message`) is dropped there, edits included. The value `"none"` makes the anchor log only, e.g. `{ "github:owner/repo@releases" = "none" }`: each event is written to the routing log of every project the anchor belongs to, with no recipient and the reason `direct: log only`, and is delivered to nobody; nothing is held for a follow-up, no ruling is owed, and it releases no item waiting on the anchor. `none` is never read as a session name. Read at each event: a reload applies it. | |
| `anchor_hub.quiet` | `{ drive = 1800 }` | Per event kind, the seconds of quiet before the follow-up notice; other kinds follow up 60 s after the first. | |
| `anchor_hub.quiet_max_hold` | `10800` | A burst that never goes quiet still reports this often (s). | |
| `anchor_hub.gmail_poll_seconds` | `300` | How often a mailbox Google will not push for is read. | |
| `anchor_hub.gmail_topics` | `{}` | Per account, the Pub/Sub topic in its OAuth client's own Cloud project. | if used |
| `anchor_hub.google_oauth_envs` | `{}` | For `anchor-hub-google-auth.py`: label to an `oauth.env` with the Google client id and secret. | if used |
| `pilot.waiting_minutes` | `60` | A notice without a ruling this long is flagged on the board. | |
| (no key) | | Since an earlier change there is no digest of automatic rulings and no `pilot.digest_hour`. A green `release.sh` result, which is logged and not delivered (the turn rule's second build): a routing-log line with no recipient and `"log_only": true`, ruled `merged` at once, and a server comment on the open items anchored to the PR. Only `release.sh`'s own report takes that path: the sender exactly `release` and a PR of the checkout's own repository (its `origin` remote). A green flag from any other sender, or with no PR, a malformed one or another repository's, is delivered as before. Only the automatic rulings are log-only: a red release, a page debt, a board relay and every other notice keep their own path and timing. An item waiting on the PR is closed by its `pr_merged` check, which releases the items `after` it, as before. Always on, and kept on the project's routing page. | |
| `pilot.own_repos` | `[]` | The fleet's own GitHub repositories: `"owner/repo"`, `"owner/*"` (every repository of that owner) or a numeric repository ID (an int or a digit string, from `gh api /repos/<owner>/<repo> --jq .id`), the form that survives a transfer to another owner: it is matched against the `repository.id` the event carries (a notification thread, a pull request's `base.repo`, an issue when GitHub includes it; a release answer has none), and an event without an ID is matched by name only. On these, anchor-hub logs a routine pull-request event and does not deliver it (the turn rule's third build): a PR opened, merged, closed or given new commits, and CI passing again. The decision is by the event's type at GitHub, never by its text, since the owner and the sessions share one GitHub account. Such an event, for a project in the pilot, becomes a routing-log line with no recipient and `"log_only": true`, ruled `merged` by `stackshot (auto)` at once and listed in the daily digest (`pilot.digest_hour`; with no digest nothing is logged instead of delivered). It still releases a held item scoped to the anchor, as the router's own `merged` would (the server's minute pass). A comment, a review, a review comment, failing CI, a reopen, any event on another repository, a project outside the pilot and an anchor in `anchor_hub.direct` are delivered as before. List only private repositories: on a public one, a PR opened by someone outside the fleet would be logged, not delivered. `[]`: none. Read at each event: a reload applies it. | |
| `router.model` | `"sonnet"` | The thin call's model (`--model`). | |
| `router.general_<scope>` | none | The agent a page with no agents of its own and no hub falls to, for a scope of `project_scopes.order` (see **The router acts on every project**). A value with a `/` is a page key whose `-main` agent is used (`"projects/example-general"`), or, when that page lists no front agent but has a `display-name:` line, its name-line session; otherwise an agent name that some page lists as a front agent. A scope with none set falls back to the default scope's; with none at all the notice goes to the project's builder or the owner. | |
| `router.watch` | none | `[[router.watch]]` rows `{page, anchor, agent}`: a standing ruling that an anchor notice on this anchor goes to the agent, for the owner. | |
| `router.quiet` | none | `[[router.quiet]]` rows `{page, agent}`: a standing ruling that in this project a non-urgent notice for (or on a Drive anchor of) the agent is held for one digest after the quiet hour, never for the owner. | |
| `router.command` | `["claude"]` | What starts the call, an argv list (the tests put a fake script here). The arguments after it: `-p --model <model> --tools "" --output-format json --no-session-persistence --disable-slash-commands --strict-mcp-config --safe-mode --setting-sources project --system-prompt <rubric>`, the message on stdin. `--safe-mode` keeps the user's hooks, `CLAUDE.md` and plugins out of the call (a thin call runs no hook). | |
| `router.timeout_seconds` | `120` | A call that takes longer is killed, and its notices go to the project's `-main` agent in the routing log. | |
| `router.max_notices` | `25` | At most this many notices in one call, the oldest first; the rest wait for the next tick. | |
| `router.max_age_hours` | `24` | A notice older than this when the router first looks is not judged: switching it on does not replay the backlog. | |
| `router.page_senders` | `["nightly-job", "size-watch"]` | The sender names (case does not matter; a notice's sender matches by prefix) of the jobs whose messages about a page, with `keeper:` in the text, the code rule `page-pass` holds for the page pass and never delivers to an agent. An operator whose own jobs post page notices lists their sender names here; an empty list turns the rule off. | |
| `router.extra_args` | `[]` | More arguments for the CLI, e.g. `["--effort", "low"]`. | |
| `router.unset_env` | `["ANTHROPIC_API_KEY", "ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN", "ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL", "ANTHROPIC_CUSTOM_HEADERS", "CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT"]` | Variables unset in the call's shell after the login profile has run (every other `ANTHROPIC_*` and `CLAUDE_CODE_USE_*` variable too), so the call runs on the signed-in lane and never on an API key. | |
| `router.login_shell` | `true` | The call starts from a login shell (`bash -l`), as a pane does. `false`: `env` directly. | |
| `router.page_debt` | `false` | The page-debt call (see **The page-debt call** above): `true` = one thin call per page per audit run, twice a day with the timer's two times, for the page debts that moved to the keeper. `false`: nothing runs. Read at each pass: a reload applies it. | |
| `router.page_debt_shadow` | `true` | `true` = the call's proposal is only logged to `<state>/router/page_debt.jsonl` and the 30-minute relay to the keeper is unchanged. `false` = live: the edit is written to the gbrain page (revision-checked), the debt settled, what cannot be applied goes to the page's own agent, else the `-main` agent (its hub's or the scope's general one for a page that lists none), and the 30-minute relay stops. Needs the gbrain CLI as the server's user. | |
| `router.live_tick_seconds` | `60` | How often the live tick takes the notices the router holds (a thin call is made only when there are some). | |
| `router.hold_notices` | `true` | Whether a notice that asks nothing of a background agent is held for its next turn instead of starting one (see **A notice that asks nothing of a background agent is held for its next turn**). `false`: every notice is delivered as before. Read at each use. | |
| `router.digest_hour` | `9` | The hour (0-23, the instance timezone) from which the day's digest of held notices is logged for each project; a digest with a notice that could not be ruled goes to its builder (see **The daily digest of held notices**); a server down then sends it when it is back the same day. | |
| `router.notes` | `true` | Live mode: a `note` on a ruling is written to the project page's timeline, once per notice. `false` = notes are ignored. | |
| `for_you.slack_command` | `""` | The For you list (see **The For you list** above): an urgent row (a due date within 24 h) is also posted once to Slack by this command, an argv with the row's text on its standard input (exit 0 = posted). Empty = no Slack path, the row only: stackshot holds no Slack posting credential of its own. | |
| `presence.minutes` | `30` | How long the owner counts as there for a pane after he last typed in it (`<state>/prompts/<session id>`, written by the prompt hook, read by stackshot; see **No automatic return or fallback goes to a front agent**). A front agent is told of an item only while he is there. A missing section uses 30; so does a value that is not a number above 0 (0, a negative, text), which is named in the config notes printed at start and at each reload (`config.notes()`) instead of being changed silently. | |
| `portal.account_id` | `""` | The Cloudflare account id, when this instance is an MCP Portal server; empty: `install.sh` skips the portal-sync unit. | if used |
| `portal.server_id` | `"stackshot"` | The instance's entry in the portal. | if used |
| `portal.token_file` | `"~/.config/stackshot/portal-token"` | The Cloudflare API token file the portal sync reads. | if used |
| `portal.catalog_file` | `""` | Where `connectors.py build` (run by `portal-sync.sh` after every sync) writes the connector catalog: the portal's entries, their upstream URLs and tools, joined with the CLI's connector names (`claude mcp list`, run in an empty directory; the `claude.ai ` name is the primary, and a server's other names there or in `~/.claude.json` are its `aliases`). The page hooks read it to see a board or page write inside a portal Code Mode script as they see the direct connector's. `""`: `<paths.state>/connectors.json`. | |
| `portal.catalog_commit` | `false` | `true`: a changed catalog is committed and pushed in the git checkout that holds it, so an instance's config repo carries the same names to every box. | |
| `auth.mode` | `"google"` | google, or off for local testing only (never in the unit). | |
| `auth.base_url` | `"https://stackshot.example.com"` | The public base URL the OAuth provider announces. | yes |
| `auth.allowed_emails` | `["you@example.com"]` | The Google accounts allowed to sign in. | yes |
| `auth.allowed_client_redirect_uris` | claude.ai and claude.com callbacks | The OAuth client redirect URIs accepted; add a portal's callback if you use one. | if used |
| `auth.ui_open` | `true` | true serves the board to whoever reaches it (an access proxy decides); false asks for the same Google sign-in. | yes |
| `auth.ui_session_days` | `30` | How many days a board sign-in lasts. Each visit (a browser opening a page, not a page's own polling) starts the days again, so a machine in use stays signed in; one not opened for that many days asks again. | |
| `auth.portal_identity` | `""` | Who the portal's machine token acts as; empty means the first allowed email. | if used |
| `client.url` | `"https://stackshot.example.com"` | Where a machine reaches the server (the server box uses its local port). | yes |
| `client.online_minutes` | `10` | A machine is online while its puller checked in within this. | |
| `client.self_update` | `true` | A client box's puller keeps the box current while it is idle: its whole checkout fast-forwarded to the server's commit, each attempt reported in its poll (`facts.updates`). In the server's config it speaks for every box (never its own); in a client box's own config, `false` is that box's veto. Live. | |
| `client.session_links` | `""` | The fleet tool's live store of session links, `<letter>/sessions.json` per machine (sala's: `"~/.local/state/salamander/fleet"`): each session's link is the `url` the tool writes for its transcript id there (board.md, the brief, the `sessions` tool). `""`: no session links. Contract: [1, Heartbeat fleet store](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#1-heartbeat-fleet-store); the same directory holds `session-registry.json`, [19, Session registry file](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#19-session-registry-file). | if used |
| `client.session_view` | `""` | The fleet tool's web view of one session, e.g. `"https://sala.example.com/#s={name}"`: on the web pages a session name with a live pane links there, `{name}` its url-quoted name. `""`: names stay text on the pages. Live. Contract: none for the page itself; the link form is the `url` of [1, Heartbeat fleet store](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#1-heartbeat-fleet-store). | if used |
| `client.lanes_file` | `""` | The fleet tool's lane record (`<session>\t<lane>` rows; `lane-default` beside it; sala's: `"~/.local/state/salamander/lanes.tsv"`), read by the `lane` done check as the tool reads it, (with `lane-signin.state` beside it, the tool's sign-in watch). `""`: no lane data, the check is refused. Live. Contract: [12, Lanes and limit state read by stackshot](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#12-lanes-and-limit-state-read-by-stackshot). | if used |
| `client.limit_file` | `""` | The fleet tool's usage-limit watch state (sala's: `"~/.claude/salamander-limit.json"`). Not read any more (it served the re-send of an unruled notice to a keeper, which went with the old routing); the key stays so an existing config loads. Contract: [12, Lanes and limit state read by stackshot](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#12-lanes-and-limit-state-read-by-stackshot). | if used |
| `client.recycle_env_prefix` | `""` | `client.py recycled` (the fleet tool's end-of-recycle hook) reads a flag it is not given from `<prefix>KIND`, `STARTED`, `FLAG_AT`, `RESULT` and `SKIPPED` in the environment (sala's: `"SALA_RECYCLE_"`; sala calls the hook once per cleared recycle flag, and `FLAG_AT`, that flag's time, makes the call cover the items flagged at or before it). `""`: the flags only. Contract: [4, Post-recycle hook](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#4-post-recycle-hook). | if used |
| `client.session_ttl_hours` | `12` | A session registered by a hook on a machine with no puller is forgotten after this long without a sign of life. | |
| `server.host`, `.port`, `.path` | `"127.0.0.1"`, `8020`, `"/mcp"` | Where the server listens and where the connector is served. | |
| `keywords.step_commands.shell_names` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language names of a shell (the English ones are PowerShell, pwsh, WSL, terminal, shell, console): a text that names one asks the owner to run something there. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.step_commands.windows_words` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language names of Windows or PowerShell: a command with one of them says where it runs. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.step_commands.run_commands` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language imperative phrases that ask the owner to run, type or paste something (run it, type it, paste it). Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.step_commands.after_command` | `[]` | A list of words: words that right after a command's closing backtick make it a command for the owner to run (the verb that follows its object; a particle may be part of the phrase). Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.step_commands.subject_markers` | `[]` | A list of words: the particles that mark a subject right before a backticked command: a session named as the subject runs it, not the owner. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.step_commands.pointer_patterns` | `[]` | A list of patterns: other-language regular expressions for a step that points elsewhere ("see the description", "see below"). Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.step_commands.pane_cues` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language run cues that may stand before a pane-form `! command` (the English ones are type, run, enter, execute, paste). Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.router.decision` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language words for a decision: a notice with one needs the owner (weak signal). Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.router.urgent` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language words for urgent: a notice with one passes the quiet hours. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.model_guards.wait_words` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language words that say an item waits (waiting, wait for): a next line with one reads as a wait. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.model_guards.overnight_words` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language words for overnight: a doing item whose next line says so reads as a wait. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.model_guards.defer_after_id` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language phrases that follow a board id and defer on it (closes, is done, after, until, before). Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.dates.month_suffix` | `[]` | A list of words: the word written right after a month number in a month-and-day date ("<month number> <this word> <day number> <day_suffix word>"). Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.dates.day_suffix` | `[]` | A list of words: the word written right after a day-of-month number (and after a number of days). Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.dates.week_suffix` | `[]` | A list of words: the word written right after a number of weeks. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.dates.later_words` | `[]` | A list of words: the words after a number of days or weeks that mean "from now". Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.dates.tomorrow` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language words for tomorrow. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.dates.day_after_tomorrow` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language words for the day after tomorrow. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.dates.next_week` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language words for next week. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.dates.next_month` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language words for next month. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.pages.board_label` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language words for the Board label of a turn's closing board line. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.pages.no_change` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language words for 'no change' in that line. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.rebullet.pronouns` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language pronouns a rewrite must not resolve into a name (matched as whole words). Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.rebullet.negations` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language negation words or endings a rewrite must not lose (matched anywhere in a word). Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.rebullet.negation_words` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language negation words matched only as whole words. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.reports.ask_words` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language words that make a report an ask (needs, please review, decide, approve). Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.draft.stems` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language words that say a step posts, sends or pastes a text (matched anywhere in the step). Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.draft.stem_patterns` | `[]` | A list of patterns: regular expressions for such words that need a condition after them (found anywhere in the step). Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.draft.standalone_stems` | `[]` | A list of words: such words that count only as a whole word, with at most one particle after them. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.draft.particles` | `[]` | A list of words: the particles that may follow a standalone stem. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.draft.ignore_phrases` | `[]` | A list of words: phrases that contain a stem but are not about posting a text (a screen share). Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.waiting.park_words` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language words for park, postpone or hold: the owner's answer with one parks the item. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.server.command_cues` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language words that say an item waits on the owner running or pasting a command. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.ask_options.particles` | `[]` | A list of words: the suffixes that may follow the last word of an option's label in a Recommend sentence (a language that glues them on): the label still counts as named. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.ask_options.refusals_after` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language words that say 'not this option' right after a label (a language that puts the refusing word after it): the option right before one is turned away; an optional `particles` suffix and spaces may sit between, and the word needs no word boundary after it. There are no English words of this kind; empty adds nothing. Live. | |
| `keywords.ask_options.refusals` | `[]` | A list of words: other-language words or phrases that say 'not this option' right before a label, letter or place (the English ones are not, no, avoid, never, skip, except, don't, instead of, rather than): the option after one is turned away. Added to the English words; empty adds nothing. Live. | |

The `keywords.*` rows are the words of another language. The matchers that read free text (a step, a notice, an owner's answer, a report) ship with English words only; an instance
that writes in another language lists its own words under `[keywords.<feature>]`, and each list ADDS to the English ones. A missing section or key is an empty list, which is the English
behavior exactly. A `words` list holds literal words or phrases (a space inside a phrase stands for any run of white space); a `patterns` list holds regular expressions. They are read
where they are used, so a reload takes a change at once, and `./stackshot doctor` names a section or key this version does not read, a value that is not a list of strings and a pattern that does not compile. The `[keywords.*]` tables are optional, and their values (the words of the operator's own language) live in the operator's private config, never in this public tree.

The code's defaults are the example's values, so a key missing from the file behaves as the table says. A config lists
every key anyway, because `./stackshot setup` writes a complete copy of the example with your values filled in.

## The client contract

A machine where Claude Code runs needs three things from stackshot, and no copy of the config:

- **A machine token.** `./stackshot client mint <machine>` on the server box writes it to
  `<secrets>/client-tokens/<machine>.token`; the server keeps only its hash. On the machine,
  `./stackshot client install --url <base URL> --token-file <file>` stores it at `~/.config/stackshot/client.token`,
  beside `client.url` (one line, the server's address).
- **The puller.** One `puller.py` per machine: a user unit on Linux (`install.sh puller` renders
  `stackshot-puller.service`, and `client install` installs and starts it), a scheduled task on Windows. It delivers
  notices and messages and reports the machine's sessions and liveness. From puller 8 it reloads itself when the
  `VERSION` in its checkout's `puller.py` differs from the one it loaded (see docs/windows.md), so a checkout update needs
  no hand restart.
- **The hooks.** The four scripts in `client/hooks/`, wired into `~/.claude/settings.json` by `./stackshot client install`
  (SessionStart, UserPromptSubmit, Stop, and PostToolUse on the connector's `create`, `update` and `complete`).

The connector itself is per account, added once on claude.ai. See [INSTALL.md steps 6 and 7](INSTALL.md#6-claude-code-on-the-server-box)
for the server box and for another machine.

### Reporting a setup run

A machine that provisions itself (a Windows PC at logon and daily) reports how each run ended, so a failed step shows
on that machine's column of the board's Machines panel. The provisioning script calls, with the machine's own token:

```
client.py setup-result --ok
client.py setup-result --failed --step "<step name>" [--reason "<short text>"]
```

It prints one line and exits 0 when the server recorded the result, 1 when it was refused or not reached (the script
decides what that means for its run; nothing is retried). Underneath it is one authenticated write:

- `POST /client/setup-result`, `Authorization: Bearer <machine token>`, body
  `{"ok": true}` or `{"ok": false, "step": "<step name>", "reason": "<short text>"}`.
- `ok` is a JSON boolean. A failed run must name its `step` (400 otherwise; an ok run keeps none); `reason` is optional. Whitespace is
  collapsed; `step` keeps 120 characters, `reason` 500.
- The machine is the token's, never the body's. The server stamps the time it received the result and keeps the latest
  one per machine, replacing the one before: `{"at": "<UTC ISO>", "ok", "step", "reason"}`, answered back as
  `{"machine": "<machine>", "setup": {…}}`.

On the board, a failed last run is a card at the foot of the machine's column: **setup · failed ✗**, then the step's
name; the reason and the time are in its tooltip. A good run shows nothing on the card. The next result replaces the last, so a fixed step clears the card at the next run.

**Update attempts.** The poll's machine facts (`POST /client/poll`, `facts`) may carry `updates`, a list of
`{"what", "from", "to", "ok", "at", "reason"}` (`at`: an ISO time or an epoch), for the helper's own updates and the
CLI's. The server keeps the last list a poll sent (at most 20 entries; a poll without the key leaves it as it was). When
the latest attempt listed for a `what` has `"ok": false`, the machine's column shows **update · failed ✗**, then the
`what` and the version it tried; the reason and the time are in the tooltip, the same way. An attempt without a boolean
`ok` counts as neither.

### Permanent session ids

The fleet tool gives every session an id that never changes (the permanent id, a UUID) and publishes `<letter>/session-registry.json` in the directory
`client.session_links` names. With that file, `sessions_registry.py` turns any reference to a session (a permanent id, a name, an alias, a
Claude transcript id) into one permanent id, or into nothing when it cannot tell (a name two sessions carry, an id it does not list: it never
picks). An open item stores its creator (`metadata.owner`), its needs entries and its observers as the permanent id where it resolves, and everything people read
(`get`, `brief`, the board, the item page, the GitHub mirror, the client API) shows the session's current name, so a rename or a fresh start
changes nothing stored. Where a session is compared (needs gates, mover checks, who sent a write) the two sides are compared as permanent ids
when both resolve, and by the old rule otherwise. A message, a notice or an inbox line addressed to a permanent id goes to the session by the name
it has now, and the inbox line keeps the permanent id it was put for, so a poll finds the session whatever name or transcript it carries when it asks. Without the file (missing, unreadable, another schema) nothing changes from before. `roles.json` keeps its name keys and gains an
`ids` map (name → permanent id). Each entry also carries `live` (additive): whether `sala sessions --json` lists the name as a session, or as an alias of one (a session keeps the names it had before a rename); the server reads sala once per build with a 4-second limit, and leaves the field out of every entry when sala cannot be read (no `[client] session_links` store, an error, a slow read). A name-line (`keeper`) entry is `retired: true` when its page has user-facing agents or its own `status` is a finished one, and a name held by several pages takes an unfinished page over a finished one. Every tool that takes a session (`sender`, `owner`, `needs`, `observers`, `to`) accepts a permanent id.

The one-time move of what is already stored: `python3 session_ids.py migrate --dry-run` (read-only) says what it would change and what it
keeps (and why). The command never writes an item. `migrate --request` writes `<state>/session-ids-migrate.request`, and the server applies
the move at its next start, before it starts any writer thread or serves a request (so nothing else writes an item meanwhile). The request is consumed
even when the registry is unreadable at that start (nothing is done): fix the registry and request again. It rewrites the creator (`metadata.owner`), needs and
observers of open items only (a closed item is a record of its time and is never touched), and writes
`<state>/session-ids-undo-<UTC time>.json` (gid, field, old, new) before it changes the first item. `python3 session_ids.py rollback <undo file>`
asks the same way (the request names the undo file) and the server puts each field back where it still holds what the migration wrote. A
failure is logged and the start goes on. A second request changes nothing. `roles.json` is written by the server's own roles sync, and the
inbox hold lists belong to the fleet tool: neither is rewritten by the migration, and stackshot only reads the hold lists (a line may be a name, a
transcript id or a permanent id). The command is refused outside the production checkout without `$STACKSHOT_CONFIG`.

The board page leaves out an agent whose `live` flag in `roles.json` is false (sala lists no session of it), on a project page's Sessions section and on the index, where an agent that still owns an open item stays. A name `roles.json` does not know, and a file written while sala could not be read, hide nothing. The agents of a finished project (its page status is one of `[project_scopes] retired_statuses`) sit in a `finished` group that starts closed. The project picker's listing is unchanged.

### Filing a question and reading the answer

A script that hits a question only the owner can answer (a headless `claude -p` run, a watcher) files it as a board item and
reads the answer later, from a shell, with the machine's token:

```
echo "<the question, a few lines>" | client.py post question --project <key|id|slug> --ask "Decide: … · Recommend: … · Stakes: … · By: …" \
    --subject "<subject>" --request-id <token> [--option "<label>=<line>" ...] [--recommended <label>] [--sender <script label>]
client.py get answer board#<n>
```

`post question` prints the new item's id (`board#N`) and exits 0; a refusal, or a server it cannot reach, is one line on stderr and exit 1.
Underneath it is one authenticated write:

- `POST /client/question`, `Authorization: Bearer <machine token>`, body `{"project", "ask", "text", "subject", "request_id", "sender"?, "options"?}`
  (`options`: `[{"label", "line", "recommended"}]`). `project` is an id, a page key or a bare slug of a page in the registry (404
  unknown, 400 ambiguous). The item is open, `needs` = `decision`, with the ask and the options, filed under the project's page,
  and owned by the project's front agent, its `-main` agent first, else the first front agent by name (409 when the page and its hub
  list none).
- `text` becomes the description, one `- ` bullet per line (a marker it already carries is not doubled), each clipped to
  `[description] line_max` characters as it reads (at a word outside any link and any naked URL, with an ellipsis; a line that is empty after
  its marker is skipped: the create gate judges it like any create). More non-blank lines
  than `[description] max_lines` less one, or more than 8000 characters, is a 400 saying so, and the script shortens it. The
  description ends with the bullet `- Filed by <sender> (a script on <machine>)`, so the item page and the GitHub issue body show
  the owner who filed it. `sender` (default `client.py`) is also kept in
  the item's `filed_by`; the machine is the token's.
- `subject` is required (400 without it, naming the instance's subject prefixes): there is no default prefix, since a question filed
  under the wrong one lands in the wrong place on the board; the create gate then checks the prefix as for the tool.
- `request_id` is required (400 without it; one line of at most 120 characters) and makes the write idempotent per `(machine, sender,
  request_id)`: the server keeps the item in its request map (`<state>/requests.json`, where a tool create's `request_id` lives), and
  the same triple again answers the same item (`"repeat": true`, the same id printed, exit 0) and files nothing. If that item has since
  been removed, the call files a new one. **the fleet tool passes one per parked question**, the same token each time that question is filed
  again, with the same `--sender`.
- It is the create tool's own code (`_create_item` in `server.py`): the subject prefix, the ask's form, the options (2 to 6, exactly
  one recommended), one decision per ask, the project page, the GitHub mirror and the page-debt rules apply as to a session, and a
  refusal is the tool's text (400). A script owes no page, and the owner is charged none: the page debt goes to the page's keeper
  (a page's own agent that is not a front agent, else the hub's or the scope's general `-main` agent) to no one when the page has none, and to no one when the keeper is the owner itself, so a burst of
  questions cannot block the owner's own board writes. The owner is told nothing; it finds the item in its queue.
- `GET /client/answer?key=board#<n>` returns the owner's answer on the item as one line, `<kind>` TAB `<chosen option's label>` TAB
  `<text>` TAB `<when>`, or an empty body while he has not answered. It is his newest `decision` (he answered the item), even when he
  commented after it; only when he has decided nothing, his newest `comment`. **A caller resumes only on `decision`** and keeps waiting on
  a `comment`. The label is empty when he picked none; line breaks in the text are ` / `; `<when>` is when he gave it, an ISO time in the
  instance's timezone. The line does not say which question it answers: an item answered before the question was filed on it returns
  that answer, so a caller files a fresh item per question. Older letter-and-number ids work. Like `brief` and `items`, it is not scoped to a machine.
- Exit codes. `client.py get answer` prints the line (nothing when empty) and exits 0, or exits 1 with the reason on stderr: no id
  given (usage; the server's own answer to an empty key is 400), an id that names no item or is not an item id (404), no token on the
  machine (401), or a server not reached (`[0]`). It never reads the project record, so it has no 503. `client.py post question` exits 1
  on a refusal (400 the tool's checks, the missing subject or request id, a text over the limits; 404 no such project; 409 no front
  agent), on no token or a server not reached, and on 503 when the project record cannot be read right now (nothing was filed).
- `GET /client/items?key=<session>` returns `{"items": [{gid, shown, subject, needs, state and state_fields (only for an item that stores a state), next, state_updated, confirmed, updated, held, list, mover, expected_by (null for a doing item), wait, overdue, step, roles, …}]}`:
  the open items the session moves, takes after the owner's answer or is named in (the board check's read). `list` is the home page's list the item is in, one of
  `"your-move"` (alive, the owner moves it), `"agents"` (alive, a session moves it) or `"waiting"` (waiting on a time, an anchor, another item or a relay), from `home.list_of` on the item
  model's reading. It is never empty: an item the page cannot place (a dead or unreadable one) is `"agents"`, as `home.classify` puts it, so the fleet tool's items sheet groups exactly as the page does.
- `GET /client/for-you` returns `{"generated", "rows": [{id, kind, item, project, what, url, urgent, ts, when, slacked}], "flags": {"board#N": "watch" | "mute"}}`
  (rows newest first; an item with no flag is in neither).
- `POST /client/for-you/seen`, `Authorization: Bearer <machine token>`, body `{"id": <row id, a whole number from 1> | "all", "sender": "<label>"}`.
  200 `{"ok": true, "seen": [<ids that left the list>], "rows": [<the rows still unseen, as in GET /client/for-you>]}` (`"all"` on an empty list is 200 with
  `"seen": []`). 400 a body that is not an object, a `sender` that is not one line of at most 80 characters, or an `id` that is neither; 401 no or an unknown
  token; 403 the caller is not the web form (`sender` not in `form_senders`, or the token's machine not in `form_machines`); 404 an id that is no row on the
  list (never made, or already seen).
- `POST /client/items/{key}/watch`, the same token, body `{"state": "watch" | "mute" | "default", "sender": "<label>"}`. `{key}` is an item as shown
  (`board%23N` in the path, or `N`) or its store id, read the way the other client routes read an id. 200 `{"ok": true, "item": "board#N", "state": "<the
  new state>", "flags": {<the whole map, as in GET /client/for-you>}}` (`default` removes the flag). 400 a bad `state` or `sender`; 401; 403 as above; 404 an unknown item.
- Ranks are retired, and `POST /client/rank` with them (the route answers the usual 404 for an unknown write). A builder starts every item handed to it at its next turn, and releases go out in merge-queue order. `update` still takes `rank` and `clear_rank` only to refuse them ("ranks are retired"), and a retired state input is refused the same way (below). At every server start `server._retire_rank_pass` removes `metadata.rank` from each open item that still stores it, whatever its value, and clears a stored state `queued` (a state that stood on the rank alone), so the item reads as derived from its fields (doing, for an item in a builder's hands). Each cleaned item that names a session in needs also gets `metadata.agent_from` set to the moment of the cleanup, so the 3-hour agent ladder counts from then and an item that sat queued for 3 hours to 3 days is not overdue and nudged at the first pass. `updated`, `state_updated`, `confirmed` and every other field stay as they were; a closed item is not touched; nothing goes to GitHub; a store with nothing to clean writes nothing, so every start can run it. The save is quiet (no mover settled, no hand-off ended). The item's History says it from its revisions: `rank cleared (was N)` and `state cleared (was queued)`. A stored `queued` read anywhere else (an old revision, a stale copy) loads as `doing` (`states.LEGACY_STATES`).
- **The old item-state inputs are retired** (`retired_inputs.py`, `server._RetiredInputs`, `server._state_apply`; `tests/retired_inputs_scenario.py`). An item stores alive or waiting directly (the item model), so `update` and `create` no longer take `state`, `clear_state`, `party`, `chase`, `end`, `proof` or `chase_note`: they are gone from the tools' signatures and descriptions, and a call that still passes one (a client's cached schema, an old script) is refused before the tool runs with one `ToolError` that names what to pass instead (`mover`, `hold` with `wake` and `wait_basis`, `after`, `wait_kind` with `wait_event`, `ask_by` and `post_wake_mover`, `needs` and `ask`; the word of a `state=<word>` is answered by what that word said), and nothing is written. An input a client sends empty (`""`, `false`, `null`) is no call and is left out. `POST /client/state` answers 400 with the same refusal and `client.py post state` prints it and exits 1; `tools/state_from_fields.py` and `tools/move_waiting_doing.py`, the two one-time jobs that used the route, are removed. `step` and `supplies` stay as they were: a `step` stores the your-step state and `supplies` the blocked one, each refused without what its state needs (`states.problems`, with what to add). A step is withdrawn with `step="none"` (like `needs="none"`): the your-step state and his open step line go in that write and the mover goes back to `then`; it is refused on an item that stores no step. A `supplies` on an item stored in another state swaps it to blocked. **What stays stored and read:** an item an older write left a stored `state` word, `party`, `chase`, `end` or `proof` on keeps them (no write drops one, a close still does): the readers take them (`states.of`, `model.derive`, `watchers`, the item page and `get`); a write that touches a field a stored state stands on (`states.STANDS_ON`) so that the item can no longer stand in it drops the word and says so in the reply (`(stored state X dropped: …)`), since no call can move or clear a state any more. An item with no stored state is read from its fields (decision → your-call, external → outside, a hold → parked, an open `after` → blocked, else doing) and shows no state word: `get`, the brief and `client.py get items` carry `state: <s>` (`state` and `state_fields`) only for an item that stores one, and the model line says alive or waiting and who moves it. **The retire pass** (`retire_fields.py`, `server._retire_one`, `tools/retire_fields_report.py`; `tests/retire_fields_scenario.py`) takes the stored state words and carry fields out of the open items: `party`, `chase` and `end` go to `wait_event`, `ask_by` and `wake` when those are empty (a verifying item's end day becomes a wake at 23:59 of that day, the end of it, as the state read it; a party the `wait_event` does not already name, and a chase day the `ask_by` does not start with, stay where they are, since a reader (the relay's text, the router's item list, the cold call) takes the `wait_event` or `ask_by` only when the `party` or `chase` is empty, and would show the other text once the field was dropped; on an item that stores a `wait_kind` the watchers read them, so its `party` stays and so does a `chase` with no `ask_by`), a verifying item's `proof` becomes the text of a `wait_basis` on its wake, `supplies` becomes a `- Needs: …` bullet at the end of the description (held, and left in `supplies`, when the description with it would pass 8 lines or 120 characters), `expected_by` and `expected_reason` are dropped; agent work in hand (the stored state doing with a session as mover: `waiting.doing_agent` reads it as kind `doing`, with no expected time, from the stored state alone), a your-step item, a step with no home and a proof outside a verifying item are held back and listed; `wait_kind` stays. The server refuses an item whose phase, kind of wait, mover, watched status, wait (`waiting.compute`: the kind, past due or not, the expected-by, within a minute for the verifying item's 23:59) or whose followers' waits (the items that wait `after` it) would read differently. The script's dry run (the default) runs the whole pass on a copy of the store and prints the counts; `--apply --sender <you>` runs it through the live server (op `retire` of `POST /client/migrate`, behind `[model] migration`) after writing an undo file; `--undo <file> --sender <you>` puts the keys back (an item a later save changed is `kept`). No write stores a state word now except `step` and `supplies`; the pass is rerun to take those out.
  **Checks for the state rules** (`tests/state_checks_scenario.py`). The owner's step hidden under an agent's state (refused since an earlier change): a create or an update is refused, and writes nothing, when the item it would leave has a first line of `next` that opens with a who-takes-it opener (`<owner>:`, an alias such as `<alias>:`, `you:`, or `the owner to …` / `<alias> to …`; never `the owner's`, `OPS-X` or "the owner approved …") while its
  state, stored or derived, is doing, blocked or verifying (one detector, `states.next_names_owner`, serves the refusal and the note). The refusal names the fix: `step=…`, or `needs=decision` with an ask (a create has no step, so it takes `needs=decision` and an ask, or a first line for the session that takes the step). Only a write that sets `next`, `needs` or the step is judged, so the mover of a legacy item can fix it step by step; a write that sets none of those (a due date, a description) to an item already in that shape is allowed. `get`, the brief and that write's reply keep the note for such an item, `next names the owner but the state is <s>: pass step=… (or needs=decision with an ask)`, so legacy items stay visible. Closed items and the quiet states (your-step, your-call, outside, parked) are never refused or noted; the terminal prompt's own row is exempt on create and update (its script stores your-step right after the create). When the first line is another session's (next keeps each writer's lines at their place), the refusal names that writer and the exits: drop the step (`dropped=[its number]`), `clear_next=true`, or `step=…` if it is truly his step; it says to mark the step done only when his own words for it are recorded on the item (the audit's N4 flags a done mark on his step without them, and never a dropped one). Re-parking: a write that moves the wake of a parked or verifying item (or a verifying item's `end`) to a
  later day with nothing else changed but the wake, the end, the hold and the cap words (a changed `wait_basis` text is a real change; a pull-in or a time of day on the same day neither counts nor resets) is counted in `metadata.repark`, any other change resets it, and the third in a row is refused (the
  item goes to the owner as `needs: decision` with an ask) unless the owner's words stand behind it (a checked `said` entry, or his answer on the item since it last moved). A verifying
  item's `end` is at most 30 days out and stands as its wait like a wake (`waiting.end_epoch`): no separate wake, no idle nudge and no stale mark before it. Stale `next`: a write by a session that is neither the owner nor named by `next`, which changes needs, the stored state, hold, wake or after and not `next`,
  sets `metadata.next_stale {by, fields, at}`, shown as `(next may be stale: <who> changed <fields> <when>)` in `get` and the brief until a write passes `next`; a `next` that names a date
  that has passed (`Thu Mar 26th`, `Mar 26th`, `2099-03-26`; paths and file names are skipped, a bare day before a counting noun such as "May 5 items" is a count, a yearless date rolls into next year only when more than 90 days past) is marked the same way. A decision asked again: a write that puts `decision` back into needs
  within 60 minutes of the owner's last answer on the item is refused unless the ask line changed in the same write (an item already on his call is not put back, so a restated `needs=decision` on it passes) (a board decision keeps the ask it answered in its answer record).
  No item is hinted to store a state any more (`states.hint` and `pages.state_hints` are gone): neither the hook's board questions nor the items read carries `state_hint`.
  A refused board write must not go unseen inside a Code Mode script (an item can sit for days that way): every row of the items read carries `roles`, the asking session's live roles on the item
  (`mover`, `then`, `needs`), and the hook's board questions (the early one and the Stop one) say them beside each item (`board#N "…" (it comes to you after the owner (you are its then))`, `(named in its needs)`, `(you are still its mover)`).
  "You are still its mover" is only for an alive item the session moves while neither keeping it nor being named in its needs (the items read lists such an item too), the hand-back to someone else that failed
  after the needs were cleared; a keeper, or a builder named in needs, moves its item by default and reads only its keeper or needs role. A follow-up sentence is added only when a listed row says "still its mover":
  the role is the server's reading now, so a hand-back that still shows it was refused. The server also logs every refused tool call to stderr, one line, the reason clipped to 300 characters
  (`refused: update item=board#N sender=<session>: Refused: …`; the tool, the item id and the sender, then the tool's own refusal text, which can quote a short fragment of the call; never the call's other arguments).
- **The item model** (`model.py`, step 1 of the redesign, the data layer: the write guards come with the next PR). An item is `dead` (closed), `alive` (someone in the perimeter
  is working: one mover) or `waiting` (nothing happens until something outside arrives: a time, an anchor's event, another item's close, or news only the owner relays). New optional
  fields in `metadata`, on `create` and `update` (the `update` tool also takes `clear_goal`, `clear_mover`, `clear_then`, `clear_wait`, and each field takes `none` to clear it): `goal` (the
  end-state, one sentence), `mover` (the owner, a session name or the project's background agent) and `then` (who takes it after the owner's answer or step), `wait_kind`
  (`time` | `anchor` | `item` | `relay`) with its input (a time is the item's `wake`, an item its `after`; an anchor `wait_anchor` and `wait_event`, a wake being its backstop; a relay `wait_event`,
  the party or event, and `ask_by`, YYYY-MM-DD) and `post_wake_mover`. They are filing edits (no clock moves, no page is owed); a close drops the mover and the wait and keeps the goal.
  `model.derive(item, now)` reads them and today's state (your-call and your-step → alive with the owner as mover; doing → alive with the agent; outside, blocked, parked, verifying →
  waiting) and returns the state, the effective mover, `then`, the effective wait and its post-wake mover, each marked stored (explicit) or defaulted (needs holds decision → the owner, else the first
  session in needs, else the project's builder, else its -main agent, never the item's creator; a relay's post-wake mover is the owner), and `conflicts` where fields cannot both stand. `get` and the brief show `goal: …` and one line,
  `model: alive · mover <who> (then <who>)` or `model: waiting on a time (wake …) · then <who>`, with `derived: mover, then` naming what is not stored. Every save records its writer on the item
  file: `written_by` (the session's name, the owner's for his board answer, or a script's label) and `written_by_id` (the session's id, or `board`), so each revision in the store's history says who wrote it. A server save with no writer (a wake, an idle mark, a done-check close) leaves both fields as they were: they name the last real writer.
- **The stored phase and the kind of its wait** (`metadata.phase`, `metadata.waits_on`, `model.stamp_values`, `server._phase_stamp`; `tests/phase_scenario.py`). An open item now also stores `phase`, `alive` or `waiting`: what the board shows it as, worked out by the reading it has always used (`model.view` of `model.derive`, so a held doing item, a wake that carries a `wait_basis` and a doing item whose open steps are all dated for a later day are `waiting`) and written by every save of an open item through the one save path (`_save`); a closed item stores none. A waiting item also stores `waits_on`, `time`, `anchor`, `item`, `relay` or `event`: the kind of the wait the board shows (`model.shown_pair`, worked out with the phase by `model.stamp_values`); an alive item stores none, and a close drops it. Both are what every reader takes the decision from: **the stored pair is the only source of whether an item is alive or waiting and of the kind of its wait** (`model.view`, `tests/stored_pair_scenario.py`). The lists, the item page, the wire `list` of `items?key=`, the `· the board lists it as waiting until …` tail of get's `model:` line and get's `named in next:` lines all go through `model.view`, which takes the phase and the kind from `metadata.phase` and `metadata.waits_on` and keeps `derive`'s details (the mover, the wake, the anchor, the post-wake mover, the basis text) wherever they fit it. There is no switch (`[model] read_stored` is gone), no check against the fields and no disagreement counter: where the fields say otherwise, the stored pair is what is shown, until the sweep restamps it (a minute at most). An open item that stores no usable pair (a new one not yet saved, a phase waiting with no kind, a value that is no phase) is shown as the writer would store it and counted in `model.UNSTAMPED` (`unstamped_reads` in `GET /audit/status`); the next save or sweep stores the pair. The writer is the one place that works the pair out from the fields (`model.view` with `check=False`, which `server._stamp_values` calls). The History comparison that recognises a bare confirm leaves the two keys out, as it leaves out `activity`. **The phase sweep keeps both true as the clock moves** (`server._phase_sweep`, `server._phase_tick`, the minute set's tick `phase_sweep` right after the anchor pass; `tests/phase_sweep_scenario.py`). A save restamps an item, but some things change what the board shows an item as without any write to it: a dated wait's wake coming within `[waiting] dated_wait_min_hours` of now, a wait_basis wake passing, midnight in the instance timezone bringing the day a dated step waits for, an `after` item closing for an item with no stored state, an anchor scoped to or taken off the project, a change of `[waiting] dated_wait_min_hours` or `[instance] timezone`. Once a minute, at the start (before the first publish) and after a config reload, the sweep works every open item's two values out again from its fields at the clock now, without the item lock, and compares them with what the item stores; only a difference goes to `_stamp_one`, which works the values out again under the lock, re-checks the file just before replacing it and writes the two keys and nothing else: no `updated`, no `written_by`, no activity or hand-off record, no For you row, no GitHub job, no notice and no History line. A session's write between the sweep's read and its replace is never overwritten (`moved`; the next minute stamps it), a closed item is left as it is, and a warm standby copy (`STACKSHOT_READ_ONLY`) neither sweeps nor writes. One publish at the end when it stamped something (a few times a day at most; most minutes write nothing). `GET /audit/status` carries a `phase_sweep` object: `runs`, `swept` (open items looked at in the last run), `stamped`, `moved`, `refused` and `disagreements` (stored pairs found different from the reading; since the start), `last_run`, `last_stamped` (epoch seconds, null until the first), `last_ms`, and `unstamped_reads` (`model.UNSTAMPED`, above). The display follows the stored pair, so the sweep is what moves it at a clock edge. **What stays on the fields on purpose:** `until`, the event, the post-wake mover and the basis text of a wait, the seven state words in get, the item page and the wire, the watched set (a stored `wait_kind`), the write guards, the hand-off, the router and the audit's filing rules, and the writer of the pair itself. `waits_on` is only a label: no update can set it, and the stored `wait_kind` is the field it always was (it is not added to an item that stores none, because a stored `wait_kind` hands the item to the watchers below). The proof is `tests/stored_pair_scenario.py`: on a store swept at the clock, every surface (the state word, the kind, the list word, the model line, get, the brief, the named-in-next lines, the wire fields, the home page, every item page, the projects page, `board.md` on both renderers, the brief's waiting-on-the-world text) is byte-equal to the same store read from the fields alone, at the clock now and at six later clocks (+30 minutes, +1 hour, +6 hours, the next midnight in the instance timezone, +3 days, +40 days), on a copy of the live store too when `PHASE_REPLAY_STORE` names one; a stored pair the fields no longer give decides until the sweep; and a reader that works the decision out from the fields, or takes the stored waiting with the fields' kind, fails it.
- **board.md and the brief say alive and waiting, not the seven states** (`Board.home_pairs`, `Board.phase_counts`, `home.wait_words` in `render.py` and, kept in step, `ui/render.py`; `tests/stored_pair_scenario.py`, `tests/verifying_apart_scenario.py`). Both count and list from the home page's own classification (`home.classify` over `model.view`, the same three lists with the same sorts), so board.md, the brief and the home page cannot disagree on a count. **board.md:** the head reads `_N open · A your move · B on agents · C waiting · machines: … · rendered by stackshot_`; the sections after the Machines, Anchor hub and Task board parts are **Your move** (with the provision line), **Waiting** and **On agents**, each item drawn in full as before; a waiting item's meta line adds `waiting on <kind>` in the model's words (`model.KIND_WORDS`: a time, an anchor event, another item's close, news only the owner relays, a fleet event); the Task board table's columns are `open · your move · on agents · waiting` (open holds every open item, and a parked item is marked `(on hold)` or `(after …)` in its cell). `agents` is the home page's On agent and Queued for release lists together, so `alive = your move + agents` and `open = alive + waiting`; a verifying item is a wait on its end date and counts under waiting, and nothing counts verifying apart or names an on-hold total any more. **The brief** (the lines a session reads at start): the first line is `Board: N open (A your move, B on agents, C waiting), D completed in 14d`, then up to five `  your move: …` and five `  waiting: … · on <kind>` lines (no state word on them), and the one counting line `  alive X (your move A, agents B) · waiting C` (the old `states:` line is gone). The `brief` tool's body finds the head of board.md up to its `## Your move` section. `get`, the item page, the wire and the `brief` tool's per-item blocks keep the seven state words until a later change retires them.
- **The stamp of items nobody has saved since** (`server._stamp_one`, the `stamp` and `unstamp` ops of the migration route, `tools/stamp_phase.py`; `tests/stamp_scenario.py`). An open item that was last saved before the stored phase carries neither key; the stamp pass writes them. The route is the migration's (`[model] migration = true`, `migration_senders`, `migration_machines`, all of the operator's), with no `begin` and no board-wide pause: the server works each item's two values out when it writes, under the item's lock, from the fields as they stand, so a session's update takes its turn and nothing planned can be stale. It writes `phase` and `waits_on` and nothing else (no `updated`, no writer, no activity clock, no hand-off record, no For you row, no GitHub job; `wait_kind` untouched), re-reads the file just before replacing it and answers `moved` if another writer got there first, and refuses a closed item. `tools/stamp_phase.py --dry-run --store <copy>` shows one row per open item (old display, stored before, shown now, stored after, whether the watchers own it before and after, `add` | `fix` | `same` | `edge`) with proof lines for the display, the watchers and the files outside the two keys, all 0, and writes nothing, the copy included; `--apply --sender <you>` asks the server for the plan, writes the undo file (fsynced) before the first write and stamps in batches; `--undo <file> --sender <you>` puts each item back where the stamp left it, or answers `kept` for one a later save restamped. A file a session saved after the stamp is told apart in the apply (printed as informational, never a failure; only a file the stamp itself changed outside the two keys fails the run), and an undo entry the run died on takes its keys from the server's pass record, not the planned ones. The same write is the phase sweep's (above), which runs it every minute for the items whose stored values the clock has left behind. Run as `uv run --no-project python tools/stamp_phase.py …`.
- **The waits' watchers** (`watchers.py`, `_watch_*` in `server.py`; `[model] watchers`, default true). They act only on an item that stores a `wait_kind`; every other item keeps today's wake pass, `after` release until the migration (`watchers.watches` is the gate those three read), so no item is moved by two mechanisms. When the wait ends the item turns alive with its post-wake mover (the hold, the wake, `after` and the wait's fields go, `mover` and `needs` name the mover, `woke` records what arrived and shows in `get` until a session writes), and the mover is told in one line (several items for one recipient in one pass are one message). A **time** wait ends at its wake (the wake pass). An **item** wait ends when the last `after` item closes (the close itself, and a catch-up in the wake pass); its backstop (`ask_by`, else the wake) passing first turns it with the note that the item is still open. An **anchor** wait ends when a notice for `wait_anchor` reaches the server (`_anchor_release`: the keeper passed or merged it, or nobody rules the project, or it is the fleet's own routine line that says merged or closed; the minute pass reads the routing log for the rest) and carries the notice's gist; its backstop turns it with "the anchor stayed silent". When the wait is set the reply says what anchor-hub already recorded on that anchor since the item's last move and whether it watches the anchor for the project. A **relay** wait comes to the owner on its `ask_by` day with the ask `Decide: has <party>'s <event> arrived? · Options: arrived / not yet, ask again on <date> / drop` and three buttons: arrived → alive with the post-wake mover (`then`); not yet → the wait again on the date he writes in the box (none readable: nothing is sent and the page says so); drop → closed as not planned. A follow-up an older write logged (`metadata.chases`, shown as `chases: n`) on a relay or anchor wait still counts, though no call logs one now (`chase_note` is retired: the wait comes back on its `ask_by` day): with two on it, the wait's next end comes to the owner as `Decide: drop it? … · Options: drop / keep waiting until <date>` instead. A moved `ask_by` or wake starts the chases over. `[model] pause = true` holds all of it and today's wake pass with it; when it is false again the due ones fire in one pass. The brief lists the relay waits as "waiting on the world".
- **The search** (`search.py`, the box in `ui/search.py`, `GET /search` in `server.py`). One box in the header of the board, a project page and an item page sends a plain GET to `/search?q=…`, behind the board's sign-in like the board. The results page groups, in this order: **Items** (subject, goal, description, next lines and ask of every item, open or closed, found by its id as well), **History** (one entry per line of the item page's History, at most 600 characters), **Projects** (the Current state of every active project page, read from the pages checkout `[paths] brain`, never from gbrain) and **Sessions** (the names the board reads, linked to the fleet tool's view while the session has a live pane, as everywhere else). A result links the item or the page. Every word typed is a prefix match (`quok` finds Quokka; a run of Hangul or Kana syllables is one word, so a stem finds the same word with a particle glued on), all words must match, and a query under two characters finds nothing. No model call: SQLite FTS5 from the standard library (a `LIKE` scan over the same table where the SQLite build has none), in `<[paths] state>/search/index.db` (derived data: dropped and rebuilt on a schema change, in memory if the file cannot be written; a few MB for a store of several hundred items and pages). Each item file's modification time and size are stamped beside its rows; the server's one item save (`_save`) asks for the item to be re-indexed a moment later in the search thread, the results page syncs before it answers (the item's text only, so a write is never missed, a file edited by hand is picked up, and the page never waits on git; its History is the thread's), the stamp of an item holds the modification time of its issue's comment copy while the mirror is on (the History is then the issue's comments), and the thread (nice 10) also runs at start (the first build reads each item's History, in chunks, newest first, the database lock held only for the writes) and every five minutes. A pass never drops more than a tenth of the stamps (a Board that read part of the store), an item whose History came back empty is retried after ten minutes, and a damaged index file is dropped and built again. A deploy restarts `stackshot.service`; until the first build is done the results page says the index is still being built.
- **The search page of the board at the root is the fleet tool's search, in the board's rows** (`ui/search.py`, `search.words` / `find` / `snippets` / `page_mtime`, `GET /search` in `server.py`; `tests/ui_search_scenario.py`). The rule is that tool's: the query is lower-cased and split on white space, with "quoted text" kept as one unit, and every unit must appear as a plain case-insensitive substring somewhere in the same entry. There is no word or prefix logic (`redesign` finds `redesigned`, and a stem finds the same word with a particle glued on), a quoted phrase must appear contiguously as written, and there is no operator syntax. The page is one list, newest first, of all four kinds (a kind label on each row: Item, History, Project, Session; an item and its History rows carry the state tag), ordered by the time of the hit: an item's last change, a History entry's own time, a project page's file time, a session's last sight. Above it **When** (1 h, 6 h, 24 h, 3 days, 7 days, All, Dates with From and To in the board's zone), **Kind** (Items, History, Projects, Sessions) and **State** (Open, Closed; it filters items and their History and leaves projects and sessions alone), each box with the number it adds with the other filters as they stand. A search started from the box is the last 7 days (items live for weeks, so the fleet tool's 6 hours would hide most of them). 25 rows show first and `Show 25 more` (a link that adds 25 and lands on the first new row) goes on to 200, past which the summary says `cut at 200`. The whole search is in the address (`q`, `when`, `from`, `to`, `kind`, `state`, `n`, with `f=1` marking a submitted form so that an unticked box is off); the filters are one plain GET form whose chips are named submit buttons (`w` wins over the hidden `when`), and a small script submits it when a box or a date changes. A History row has no author on its who line: the author is a git read, which the results page never makes. A search that finds nothing says how many matches the window leaves out and which unticked boxes hold more.
- **The item page on the model** (`itempage.py`, `Board.item_html` in `render.py`). The goal is the title (the subject when there is none); one state line says the state in words (`Alive · mover X · since <when> (<age>)`, `Waiting on <what> · watched by <the scheduler | the server | the anchor module> · <until | ask by | backstop> <when> · then <mover>`, `Done <date>: <outcome>`); beside it the latest History entry says `updated <age> by <who>: <what>` (the clipped, escaped `what`; there is no "Where it stands" section any more). The ask, its buttons and the answer box show only while the item is alive with the owner as mover (an open item he does not move has the comment box in the same place, open, under the state line; the Comments fold keeps the list of comments only; the answer route is unchanged). Next shows every line, numbered, each `<mover>: <action>` (an unnamed line gets the item's mover); none is folded. A waiting item shows its wait and its chases. The sidebar holds project, mover and then, priority, expected by or wake, anchors and the GitHub issue (and the For you control); today's other fields (owner, agent at work, due, confirmed, work, after, observers) sit in a closed "More details" fold. The page runs: title and state line; Your step, the ask and its answer box, or the comment box; Next; Background; History; Comments. Background is the item's `description` (the field and the tool parameter keep their name), a fold (open): what the item is, where it lives, what done means, never steps. The write guard says so: on an item that is his to move (he is the stored mover, its state is your-step or your-call, stored or derived, or its needs hold his decision), a description write that adds a numbered line (`1.`, `2)`, also after a `- `) or a `! ` line outside a fenced block is refused, pointing at `step`; it is judged on what the write adds (a typo fixed beside an old line passes, as for commands), and a backticked command or a shell fence is refused on your-step with him as the mover as before. An agent's own items keep ordinary multi-step descriptions. History (one line per revision with its author, `written_by`) and Comments are folds, closed by default.
- **The board's pages are drawn by `ui/`** (`ui/`, the routes in `server.py`). The home (`/`), an item page (`/items/<id>`), a project page, the Projects page (`/projects`), Closed, Search and Runners are drawn by `ui/render.py`, `ui/search.py` and `ui/runners.py`; `render.py`, `search.py` and `runners.py` at the top keep the data side (the markdown and the brief, the search index, the runners snapshot and switch).
