Every address a page writes (link, form action, redirect, script fetch, paging, search form, the runners refresh) goes through `ui.under()`, which adds the prefix `ui.PREFIX` (empty, so an address is the bare root address). **Shared at the root:** the write routes (answer, watch, Mark seen, Clear and Undo on Closed, the runners switch) are one handler each, change the one store and redirect back (a form's `back` is followed only when it names a page of the board); the JSON endpoints and files (`/answer-tokens`, `/audit/*`, `/receivers.json`, `/client/*`, `/ui/*`, `/routing/*`, `/prefs/panel-order`, `/landed/seen`, `/runners.json`, `/render-stamp`, icons, font, manifest) have one address each. Sign-in needs nothing new (the cookie has path `/`; a visitor sent to `/ui/login` comes back to the address asked). The home at `/` is drawn on request and kept for 30 s (dropped at once by a publish or a write from a page). `tests/board_pages_scenario.py` checks the pages, the writes and where they return, and the sign-in return.
- **The side panel's Projects section** (`projects-panel` in `ui/render.py`, drawn by `Board.panel_blocks` like the Runners section, a fold with a grip, last by default) is the way to the Projects page: it replaced the lone "Projects" link that sat above the sections. One card per scope, in the Runners cards' look: the scope's name, its count of projects ("2 projects"; archived ones are counted, and the card's hover text gives the split, "1 active, 1 archived") and a link to that scope's group on the page, `/projects#grp-<name>` (the name lower-cased, anything but a letter or digit a dash; `Board.scope_anchor`; two scopes whose names come to the same slug keep distinct ids, the first the plain slug and a later one `-2`, `-3`…, the same on every draw). The scopes and the counts come from the same lists the Projects page draws (`Board.scopes`, `Board.project_keys`), so a new scope shows up here by itself; a scope with no project shows 0 and, having no group on the page, a link that lands at the top; the page's first row for a scope carries the id. A click on a link in the open phone drawer closes it only for a plain same-origin link with no target, so a link that opens a tab or leaves the board keeps the drawer. `tests/board_pages_scenario.py` and `tests/home_scenario.py` check it.
- **The order of the side panel's sections** (Machines, Runners, Anchor hub, Audit, Projects) is the signed-in person's own and is kept on the server, so a PC tab and a phone show the same (`uiprefs.py`; `<state>/ui-prefs.json`, `{"users": {"<address>": {"panel_order": [...]}}}`, written whole to a temporary file, fsynced and swapped in with `os.replace`, under a lock; a file that does not parse is moved aside to `ui-prefs.json.bad-<epoch>` and the settings start empty). Each section's header has a grip: dragged with a pointer (mouse, touch, pen) an orange (accent) drop line shows where it lands; clicked, or focused and Enter / Space, it opens a menu (Move up, Move down, Move to top, Move to bottom; the moves that do not apply are disabled), the way for the keyboard and a phone. Each move is announced in a live region ("Runners moved to position 2 of 5") and saved at once with `POST /prefs/panel-order`, body `{"order": [...]}` or `{"reset": true}`: signed in (403 otherwise), from the board's own origin (403), a JSON body of at most 4 KB (415, 413, 400). Names the page does not have are ignored; the names sent (the sections the page shows) take the places they hold in the person's stored full order, so a section the page did not draw keeps its place; on reading, a section the stored order lacks is put back right after its default predecessor (first when none), so a release that adds or removes a section needs no migration; a settings file that cannot be read (not a missing or unparsable one) is never replaced by an empty one: a save answers 503 and the script says it was not saved; an order equal to the default is stored as no entry. The page is drawn in the default order (the home is one kept draw for everyone) and the order is put in on the way out for the signed-in person (`server._panel_ordered`, `ui.render.apply_panel_order`, each section wrapped in `<!--ps:id-->` markers), so the saved order is in the first paint and nothing moves on load. There is no reset button: the grips move every section, and moving them back is the way to the default order (the `{"reset": true}` body stays for a script). Every section's first block sits the same 8 px under its title, with the grip clear of it. `tests/panel_order_scenario.py` checks it; `tests/panel_order_check.py` is the manual browser check (1280 and 390 px, light and dark; `PANEL_ORDER_SHOTS=<dir>` keeps screenshots).
- **The saved color combinations of the color tool** (`colorcombos.py`, the routes block in `server.py`, `tests/color_combos_scenario.py`). One list for the whole instance, oldest first, kept in `<state>/color-combos.json` (`{"combos": [{"id": "<8 hex>", "name", "values", "saved": <epoch>, "via": "board" | "sala-web"}]}`; written whole to a temporary file, fsynced and swapped in with `os.replace`, under a lock; a file that does not parse is moved aside to `color-combos.json.bad-<epoch>` and the list starts empty; a file that cannot be read, or that does not parse and cannot be moved aside, is a 503 and is left as it is). A combination carries all 14 keys, each `#rrggbb` in lowercase hex: `bg` page background, `card` cards, `fg` body text, `mut` muted text, `line` lines, `ibd` button outlines, `link` text and links, `solid` solid fills, `onsolid` text on solid fills, `labbg` small label fill, `labfg` small label text, `labbd` small label outline, `disbg` disabled fill, `disfg` disabled text; the server runs no contrast check (the app that applies a combination does). A name is NFC, stripped, 1 to 40 characters with no control, format or line-separator character (Unicode categories Cc, Cf, Zl, Zp: a tab, a line break, a zero-width space, a bidi override), and unique when case is ignored: a save under a name the list has replaces that combination and keeps its id. At most 50 combinations (a new name past that is 409 "the list is full, delete one"); a request body is at most 4 KB. Routes for the board's own page, signed in and from the board's origin like `/prefs/panel-order` (403 otherwise, a read too), JSON only (415), size (413): `GET /colors/combos`, `POST /colors/combos` (`{"name", "values"}`) and `POST /colors/combos/delete` (`{"id"}`; an id the list does not have is no error). Routes for the fleet tool's web server, by the machine token as the other `/client` routes (401 without a valid one; 403 unless its machine is in `[project_agent] form_machines`): `GET /client/colors/combos`, `POST /client/colors/combos` and `POST /client/colors/combos/delete`, whose body adds `"sender": "sala-web"`, a label that must also be in `form_senders` (the same gate as the For you writes; 403 otherwise). Every answer is `{"combos": [...], "max": 50}` with `Cache-Control: no-store`; an error is `{"error": "<a plain sentence>", "field": "<the failing key, name, values, id or sender, else null>"}` with status 400 (bad name or values), 403, 409, 413, 415 or 503, except the machine routes' 401, which is the plain text of every `/client` route. On a read-only standby copy a post is the usual 503. The paths are not under `/prefs/` (a post there drops the kept home page) and the two-segment `/client/colors/combos` paths do not meet the single-segment `/client/{what}` routes.
- **The home's count cards are 60 px tall at every width** (`nav.hcards` / `a.hcard` in `ui/render.py`): a label never wraps (`white-space:nowrap`, an ellipsis as the last resort), and where the full label does not fit the card it shows its short form, chosen by the width of the cards' column with a container query (`container:hcards/inline-size` on the nav, `@container hcards (max-width:680px)`), not by the window: with the side panel pinned the column is 288 px narrower than the window. Today only Queued for release has one ("Queued"; the rest of the label stays in `span.more`, clipped, so a screen reader still reads it all). Before, the wrapped label made the grid stretch every card to 80 px. `tests/ui_kit_scenario.py` checks the markup and the CSS; `tests/hcard_height_check.py` is the manual browser check (1280, 800 and 390 px, light and dark, panel open and closed, card heights by `getBoundingClientRect`; `HCARD_HEIGHT_SHOTS=<dir>` keeps screenshots).
- **The home's four lists are grouped by scope** (`Board.scope_groups`, `Board.scope_sections` and `Board.home_section` in `ui/render.py`, with `ui/groups.py`; `tests/home_groups_scenario.py`). The Closed page's New and Last 14 days sections draw the same subsections through the same two methods. Each of Your move, On agent, Queued for release and Waiting draws its rows in one subsection per scope, in [project_scopes] order (read through `projects.scopes()`, as the Closed page reads it), under the scope's name and its row count in the Closed page's `h3.iscope` heading (`<details class="fold sfold hscope" data-fold="home.your-move.home" open><summary><h3 class="iscope">home <span class="count">3</span></h3></summary><ul class="ir-rows">…`): a native `<details>`, the project page's list fold, so a tap on the heading folds the scope's rows. A subsection renders open, also inside a list that starts closed; each carries its own `data-fold` key (`home.<list>.<scope>`, `closed.new.<scope>`, `closed.completed.<scope>`) so the live swap on home carries a fold the viewer closed across a refresh, and nothing is remembered across a reload (home's fold script stores no state). A row's scope is `Board.landed_scope` of its project's board row (the project's mark or `derive_scope`; an item with no project is in the default scope), the reader the Closed page uses, not the older `proj_scope`. A scope with no rows in a list draws no subsection, and a list with no rows keeps its one empty line. Inside a subsection the list keeps its own sort (Your move and On agent oldest first, Waiting by end date); which lists start open, the lists' own counts, the count cards, board.md and the project pages (which draw `project_items`, not this) are unchanged. A row whose scope is not one of the configured scopes is not dropped: it sits in a last subsection under the scope's own name (several such, by name). The rows still hide the instance's bracketed subject tag (`[work] `, `[home] `) (`Board.bare`).
- **A row is a card with a colored left edge, and home and Closed name the project above the title** (`row` and the stylesheet in `ui/kit.py`, `Board.home_row`, `Board.done_row`, `Board.landed_html` and `Board.project_line` in `ui/render.py`; `tests/ui_kit_scenario.py`, `tests/rows_background_scenario.py`). Every list that draws the shared row (home's four lists, the Closed page, a project's items, Search) shows each item as its own card (the board's quiet border, 10px corners, a 12px gap between cards) with a 3px edge on its left in the list's color (`data-st` on the `<li>`): orange Your move, blue On agent, purple Queued for release, grey Waiting, green for a close on the Closed page's New section; Last 14 days, Search and a project page's closed items keep the plain edge. Home's rows and the Closed page's rows (New and Last 14 days) open with a small quiet line naming the item's project, a link to the project's page (`.ir-proj`, drawn from `project_html` of `ui.row`); a project page's own rows leave the line out, and an item with no project has none.
- **On the board at the root the Landed page is the Closed page** (`Board.landed_html`, `Board.home_counts` in `ui/render.py`, `GET /closed` in `server.py`). The fifth home card says "Closed": its number is the items closed in the last 14 days (done, dropped and superseded alike, `len(Board.done)`), and beside it a small badge "N new", N the closes of agents that the owner has not cleared yet (`Board.landed_count`, what the Landed card used to count): all of the 60 days `landed.py` keeps an uncleared close, not only the last 14, so the badge can be larger than the number, as the Landed card's count was (the item is read from all the board's rows, so a close older than 14 days shows its own title, outcome and reason, and leaves the New section only when cleared or past the 60 days); no badge at 0. The badge keeps the card at 60 px: where the card is narrow it reads "N" alone (" new" stays in the markup, clipped), chosen by the card's own width with a container query. The page it links to is titled Closed and has two sections: **New** (the uncleared closes of the last 60 days, grouped by scope and project, with Clear and Clear all, as the Landed list was) and **Last 14 days** (the paged list that was the Completed section; `#completed` is still its anchor). The old address `/landed` (any `?page=N` kept) answers a 301 to `/closed`, never cached; the Clear, Clear all and Undo posts keep their `/landed/clear` and `/landed/unclear` addresses (they are not pages), and a form post without scripts returns to `/closed`. `tests/ui_kit_scenario.py` checks the card with the badge shown and hidden, `tests/board_pages_scenario.py` the route and the redirect, `tests/hcard_height_check.py` the card heights with the badge.
- **The answer box of the board** (`ui/render.py` `_cards_html`, `_CARDS_CSS`, `_CARDS_JS`, `Board.answer_html`, `Board.home_ask`; `server._split_letter`; `tests/board_pages_scenario.py` section 2b). On an open decision the item page and each Your move row on the home draw **one card per option**, a radio input styled as a card (`name=choice`, so the form posts the field it always did and works with the script off), showing the option's label, the orange *recommended* badge on the recommended option and nothing else for it, and the option's **whole text** (the one-line cut and the per-option button are gone). A question with **three or more options letters its cards A, B, C** (in the order the owner stored the options, the order a session writes its ask in, so "Recommend: B" and the card lettered B agree; the recommended card keeps its badge wherever it sits) and the radio's value is `B · <label>`; two options (Approve and Decline, Yes and No) carry no letters and keep the recommended one first. The server takes the lettered value (`_split_letter`: the letter must be the label's stored place among three or more options, tried before a bare-label match, else the answer reads as stale): `metadata.answers[].choice` stays the bare label, the letter is kept beside it as `letter`, and History, the saved note and the notices read `B · <label>`. A picked card shows a filled circle, a 2 px accent border and a tint (one channel per meaning: recommendation is the badge only). An optional **Note** box sits below the cards and one **Send** is the only way to send; the page script greys it, with the hint "Pick an option or write a note" beside it, until a card is picked or the note has text, and a second tap on the picked card clears it (without the script Send is always on and the server answers "Nothing sent" to an empty form). A card picked with a note sends the option plus the note; a note alone is his answer as a note. The Ask block no longer lists the options while the box is drawn; for a viewer with no box (signed out) the list stays as the page's read-only copy. A decision with no stored options keeps its Yes and No (as cards); a comment-only box and a your-step box are unchanged.
- **History after a decision lists the options whole** (`ui/render.py` `Board.item_log`, `Board.decision_made`). Where the decision left the needs, History shows "the options were:" and one short bullet per option, its label, the recommended mark and its whole sentence, with the one he picked marked "picked"; a note sent with no pick adds "no option was picked". The options come from the answer record (`metadata.answers[].options`, written by `server._answer` with the answer, so they outlive the options being cleared off the item), else from the version of the item before the decision; a record without either reads as it did. Next keeps its short "picked <label> (<sentence>)" line. The live refresh (`_LIVE_JS`) does not reload or swap a page whose answer form holds a picked card or a typed note, also when the new page's frame differs after a deploy (`tests/decision_pick_check.py`).
- **The board's buttons and links: one orange accent, two levels of button** (`ui/render.py` `_BTN_CSS` and `Board._css`; `tests/button_set_scenario.py`, which computes the contrast from the stylesheet's own tokens). `--orange` is the one orange of every page: `#ad4608` on the light theme, `#f0a06a` on the dark. `--acc` (the accent), `--call` (the "Your move" card's border and the "your call" markers) and `--t-you` (the "Your move" chip) are that token, and the tints `--t-you-bg` (12% over the card) and `--selbg` (the picked card, 8%) are `color-mix()` of it, so no other orange is written anywhere; `--on-acc` is the label colour on a fill of it (white on light, near-black on dark). The favicon and the app icons keep their own, lighter mark colour (`icons/`, `server.py`'s `_ICON`; the scan lets that one hex stand only inside an icon). `tests/orange_scenario.py` scans the source and the rendered pages and fails on any other orange, and recomputes the contrast below. The text links, the focus rings, the picked option card (its border, circle and tint) and the pressed Watch / Mute / Default button read it; a link is 5.5:1 on the light page and 8.6:1 on the dark one. **The main button** is `.b.main`, the composer's **Send** (the answer box, the comment box, a step's box, the home rows), one per form: a solid `--acc` fill and an `--on-acc` label (5.7:1 light, 7.8:1 dark), its fill moving toward `--fg` on hover (a pointer that can hover only) and when pressed, kept while it is busy ("Sending…"), and at 50% opacity, still visible, while it is disabled. **The secondary button** is the plain `.b` and every other button (Clear, Clear all, Undo, seen, Copy, Watch, Mute, Default, Reload, the title bar's icon buttons): a 1 px outline in `--ibd` (3.6:1 on the light page, 5.7:1 on the dark), no fill, 13 px text at full strength (`--fg`, no longer the muted `--mut`), 32 px tall (44 px on a touch screen), 8 px corners; hover firms the outline to `--fg` and adds a light fill. The status colours keep their own tokens and are not the accent: `--run` the blue of a running or live state (the On agent badges, the Runners counts and dots, the History dots of a completed or reopened item), `--warn` the amber of a waiting or due warning, `--p1`, `--ok`, `--relay` and the tags' pairs in `ui/kit.py`. The Audit card's **Audit now** is a text link in `--acc` (a `<button id="audit-now">` without the `.b` class, in `_RUN_CSS`), not a button of either level. A row's title link (`--fg`) and the muted who / age links stay neutral and turn orange on hover.
- **The color tool: a Colors button to try a whole dark color combination** (`shared/colortool.js` is the tool, one shared source; `ui/colortool.py` is stackshot's side: its settings and the two script blocks the pages carry, hooked into `_APP_HEAD` and `_FAB_PAIR` in `ui/render.py`, with the mount `#panel-colors` that `_panel_frame` draws; `tests/color_trial_scenario.py` and the Colors block of `tests/phone_width_check.py`). On the dark theme a button, a bare two-color circle (no text and no frame: the live page color on its upper left half, the live solid fill on its lower right, split by a diagonal line; the button is named Colors by its `aria-label`), sits at the very bottom of the side panel, outside the sections that move and clear of the reload and Back to top buttons. It opens a panel upward from itself: `position: fixed` in the page body, above the reload and Back to top buttons, with its own scroll when it is taller than the screen (it fits a 360 px phone). The panel hangs from the button. At its bottom, beside the button, one box holds Presets (Live now, Old orange, Two oranges, Radix, Primer: one click sets the neutrals and the oranges of that scheme; Live now is the dark look the board ships; Old orange is the same look with the small labels as orange fills with dark text, so its click sets those three label fields) and Saved combinations together; above it a Fields button folds the 14 fields away, and opening it grows the panel upward, away from the button (the panel is then scrolled to its end, so the Fields button, the presets and the saved list stay in view). The folded part is a stack of boxes: Surfaces (page background, cards, lines), Text (body text, muted text, text and links), Buttons (button outlines, solid fills, text on solid fills, disabled fill, disabled text), Small labels (fill, text, outline). The Preview box (a Send, a disabled Send, a button, a disabled button, a "Your move" tag, a recommended badge, a link) is outside the fold, under the Fields button, so it stays in view. The fields start folded and the choice is kept in this browser. Each box heading keeps the side panel's section style (small uppercase) but is in the link color, the live text and links field, and the fields under it are indented, so a heading is not mistaken for a field. Reset returns to what is shipped: the dark default of every page (one orange `#f0a06a` for links and fills, the near-black `#1f1f21` on the fills, the small labels orange text `#f0a06a` on a dark brown fill `#3a2619`, and the disabled buttons a flat grey fill with grey text). The 14 fields are each a native color input and a hex box that apply at once. Status colors are not in it. A field left alone is the shipped value or follows the others: cards and lines follow a set background, the label on a solid fill is chosen for reading (dark or white), the small label follows the text orange, the disabled pair follows the Send faded to half. While nothing they follow is set, those five are the shipped values; once the text orange, the page or cards is set the small label is worked out from it, and once the solid fill, its label or the page is set the disabled pair is the Send faded to half. **The look you apply is kept in this browser only** (the `localStorage` key `stackshot-color-trial`, a map of the fields that are set, `#rrggbb` in lowercase; an older `{text}` is read as the text and links field; whether the Fields fold is open is kept beside it under `stackshot-color-trial:fields`, `1` when open and absent when folded, the default). It sets custom properties inline on `<html>` and two classes (`ct-on` solid fills, `ct-wl` a lighter label hovers toward black) and leaves the shipped stylesheet and its tokens as they are (`--orange` keeps its value; the text orange is read through `--orange-text`, the solid through `--orange-solid`, the label on it through `--on-solid`). The shipped dark look is the stylesheet's own: `--orange` `#f0a06a`, `--on-acc` `#1f1f21`, `--ibd` `#929190`, and five dark tokens (`--lab-bg`, `--lab-fg`, `--lab-bd`, `--dis-bg`, `--dis-fg`) with the dark rules in `ui/render.py` that draw the orange small labels and the disabled buttons from them; the tool sets those five inline once a look touches them (a small-label field or one it follows, a disabled field or one it follows). `tests/dark_default_scenario.py` pins that the dark default is the `old` preset of `shared/colortool.js` field by field except the three small-label fields (orange text `#f0a06a` on `#3a2619`, outline `#3a2619`) and that the light theme's token block (page, text, lines, cards, the orange and the label on it, the input tokens) keeps its values, and `tests/dark_default_check.py` is the same proof in a browser, by hand. The light theme is untouched: nothing is drawn or applied there. `tests/orange_scenario.py` reads the pages without the tool's script blocks and skips `shared/colortool.js`, which carry the preset oranges, and only those. **The gate** runs in the page on the combination as it resolves (a preset, a saved one and a stored one included; the server runs none). A change that breaks one of these pairs is not applied, and the panel says "Not applied" with the pair and its ratio: body text on the page 11:1 (the background gate: a page lighter than about `#303030` is refused) and on cards 7:1, muted text on the page and cards 4.5:1, button outlines on the page and cards 3:1; a stored look that fails them is cleared at load, its surface and text fields. These pairs are applied with a note and the ratio instead ("Below its limit"), and a preset that has one is marked: text and links on the page and cards 4.5:1, solid fills against the page and cards 3:1, text on a solid fill 4.5:1 at rest, hovered and pressed, a small label's text on its fill 4.5:1 and its outline against cards 3:1, the disabled text on its fill 3:1, lines against cards 1.15:1. The Radix preset's white label is applied as given and is marked (2.97:1 at rest). With `gate: "warn"` (an app's choice, not stackshot's) the first six pairs are only notes with their ratio too, a saved, stored or typed combination always applies and a stored look is never cleared. The dark disabled rule of the stylesheet gives every disabled button on the page, the panel's own two disabled preview buttons included, the flat fill and text of the two disabled fields at full opacity, the idle Send included; the light theme keeps its faded disabled buttons. **Saved combinations** are kept on the server, one list for the instance and shared with the fleet tool's web page, through `GET /colors/combos`, `POST /colors/combos` and `POST /colors/combos/delete` (see the paragraph above): the panel reads the list when it opens, Save stores the 14 resolved values under a name (the same name, case ignored, replaces it in place), a saved name applies in one tap (through the gate) and Delete asks once more. The page's only requests are those three, from the board's own address; a refusal (not signed in, the list full, a bad name, the file unreadable, offline) shows the route's message in the panel and never changes the look that is applied. **The shared-source contract.** `shared/colortool.js` is one file that more than one app draws: its first line is `/* colortool v2 */`, it carries no placeholder and no name of an app, and another app pins a verbatim copy of it (not an edited one) and passes its own settings in a `cfg` object. The file changes only with a version bump (its first line and `ColorTool.version`), and the apps that pin a copy are told, so each takes the new version on purpose. It defines `window.ColorTool` with `version`, `applyStored(cfg)` (in the head: injects `cfg.css` once and applies the stored look before the first paint; draws nothing), `init(cfg)` (at the end of the body: draws the button in `cfg.mount` and the panel in the body), `remount(mount)` (moves the button into a new mount element after the app re-rendered its own; the panel and its state stay) and `engine(cfg)` (the pure functions and constants, for tests). The `cfg` fields are `storageKey` (the `localStorage` key; the Fields fold is kept under that key plus `:fields`), `shipped` (the 14 values in use with nothing set, an object or a function called on every apply and never cached; the five that follow others, the three small labels and the disabled pair, are the app's own values while nothing they follow is set and are worked out from the live fields once one is, so an app whose shipped label or disabled look is not what that rule gives still sees its own in the panel; a key left out or not `#rrggbb` is always worked out), `vars` (`{field: [custom properties]}` set inline when the field is set; cards and lines also with a set background), `onApply(resolved, stored, set, addClass, engine)` (runs on every apply while dark, Reset and Live now included, after the tracked properties and classes were removed; `set` and `addClass` are tracked, so Reset removes what they set), `isDark()` (default: `data-theme`, else the system scheme), `gate` (`"refuse"`, the default, or `"warn"`), `css` (an extra stylesheet, injected once), `mount` (a selector or an element), `preview` (trusted HTML of the preview strip; without it that box is left out) and `api` (the saved list's base URL; without it that box is left out). Stackshot's settings are in `ui/colortool.py`; `deploy.sh` restarts the server for a change under `shared/`.
- **Next is a checklist** (`steps.py`: the list retains all actions already taken or to be taken on the item, as a checkbox list, taken ones checked and slightly dimmed; open items start the list now, a dropped step stays, struck through). *Storage:* `metadata.next` stays what it was, the text of the open lines, so the brief, the notices, the home rows, the GitHub mirror, `get`, the write guards, the router and the search read it untouched. Beside it `metadata.steps` is the checklist, a list in the order the steps were written of `{text, status: open | done | dropped, by, at}` (a step is a line of next as `nextlines.split` groups it: a top-level line with the indented lines, notes and fenced blocks under it; each open step also knows its writer, `nextlines`' record). `next` is derived from it: the open steps joined; an update that writes `next` keeps the text exactly as written and the steps follow it. A write path that knows nothing of steps (a test, the migration, an old client) only changes `next`; `steps.view` lines the stored steps up with the text it finds (a step whose line is gone reads as dropped, with nobody and no time; a new line is an open step at the end). An item with no `steps` yet has the checklist its next lines give, all open: the list starts from now, with no backfill from History. *Writing:* `update(done=[…], dropped=[…])` marks steps, each entry a 1-based position in the checklist (done and dropped steps count) or the exact text of an open step's line; a marked step keeps its place and records who marked it and when. `done` and `dropped` mark any open step, whoever wrote it, and the marked line leaves `next` with its writer (`nextlines.prune`), except that `done` on one of the owner's steps (a line that opens with his name) while the item asks him is refused (only his answer ticks it). An update replaces only the sender's own lines of `next`, so only a sender's own open step can leave `next` unmarked: when the sender's `next` (or `next="none"`) no longer lists one of its own open steps and `done` and `dropped` do not name it, the update is refused, naming the step, its position and the call to retry with in one line (`done=[n]`, `dropped=[n]` or `reword={n: "<new text>"}`; the other writers' lines are not the sender's to answer for); `update(reword={n: "<new text>"})` gives an open step of the sender's own other words: a mapping from a step (a position, or the exact text of its first line; a JSON key is the position as a string) to its new text, the step keeping its number, its place and its open state, its line of `next` (`nextlines.reword`) and its `step_proof` following it, and `next` in the same call, when it is passed, listing the new text (else the line is changed for it); refused: a step that is done, dropped or not there, another session's step, the owner's, a step also in `done` or `dropped`, a new text of several steps or one another open step already reads, a new text that is the old one with words added (the rule below for a rewritten line holds for it) and a new text a line of `next` would be refused for; `clear_next=true` is a deliberate wipe: every line it removes becomes a dropped step, by the sender. A `next` that only adds or reorders open steps works as before (the open steps take the new order, in the places open steps held; new ones go at the end). `done` or `dropped` without a `next` leaves `next` as the open steps left. A step still listed in `next` while the same call marks it is refused; one written again after an earlier call marked it is a new step. Closing marks every step still open, by the closer (done when the outcome is done, dropped otherwise; marks passed with the close win for their steps; the server's own closes mark the same way) and keeps the checklist as the record (`get` lists it, the page shows it); reopening drops the steps an older close left open. The items closed before are marked by the server itself, once at every start before it serves (`_close_marks_pass`: done for outcome done, dropped otherwise, by "stackshot (backfill)" at the close time, no mirror write, idempotent, one log line); `tools/close_marks_backfill.py` is its read-only report (dry run, `--json`, `--config <copy>/stackshot.toml`). A marks-only write counts as a change of `next` (it owes the page like one). *the owner's step made by the server (`owner_steps.py`, `server._owner_steps_on_save`, `tests/owner_steps_scenario.py`):* when an item waits on him (needs decision with an ask, or state your-step), the server adds the open step `<owner>: <what to decide>` (the Decide part of the ask, or the step's one line) to the checklist, written by the server so no session's `next` replaces it; a `<owner>:` line a session wrote that says the same as the ask or the step is adopted (recorded as the server's step, no second beside it), while a `<owner>:` line about something else (a session's "review PR 12") stays the session's own and the server's step is added beside it; a changed ask rewords it (the old dropped, the new added). His board answer ticks it as done by him at the answer's time, showing the option after an arrow (`<owner>: <decide> → <option label>`, or his quoted words cut at 60 characters); his Done on a your-step ticks it the same, and so does his note on a your-step (shown as `→ "<his words>"`; the lines a session wrote stay open); a second answer adds nothing. A session cannot tick it (an update whose `done` names it is refused while the item, as that update leaves it, still asks him; `done` together with a withdrawal in the same call is no refusal) but can withdraw what asks him (`clear_ask`, needs without decision, a state that no longer waits on him): the server drops its step with it. `dropped` on the step while the ask stands is undone: the server adds the step again. The two sources agree by a check on every save (the stored needs, ask, state and step still decide whether the item waits on him; the checklist is made to follow them), so an item that asked before this build gets its step on its next save. *the owner's step:* the item page's Your step block shows the summary line (`step`), then his own open Next lines (the steps whose first line opens with his name, or with none) in full, numbered, each command in a code block with a Copy button under its line (`step_commands` renders them); Next below stays whole, so it reads on its own. His Send with an empty box (or the old Done button) on a your-step item checks every open step of his, the ones the block shows, as done by him; a note ticks the server's step with his words and leaves the lines a session wrote open, and the session that takes the item marks them when it judges the step finished. *Reading:* `get` and the home page show the open steps (`next:`, the first open step first) and, once a step is done or dropped, `checklist:` with every step's position, status, who and when (the first line, clipped); an update's reply says `(step N done: …)`. History says `step done: …` and `step dropped: …` (the GitHub mirror's comment the same). The item page's Next fold draws the finished steps first, done and dropped together in the order they were marked, then the open steps in their planned order (`steps.drawn_order`; his decisions on the board, option picks and Approve/Decline/Yes/No/Done answers, are drawn among the finished rows by their time as `<owner>: picked <label> (<option line>)` rows of their own, `decided on the board · when`, with no position; drawing only: the stored steps, `next`, `get`'s positions and the positions `done` and `dropped` take keep the order the steps were written). It is a list of steps with CSS boxes (not form inputs), so it stays one column at phone width: open ones an empty box, done ones a checked box in the muted colour with `who · when` under the text, dropped ones struck through with `dropped · who · when`; each carries a visually hidden To do / Done / Dropped for a screen reader. A closed item shows its checklist too.
- **Next's write checks** (`next_rules.py`, `server._gate_next`, `_update_unlocked`, `_create_item`, `_report_note`; one step is one action with one named actor; nothing leaves the list; an open item has an open step; a step is an action, never a status). Only a session's write through the `create` and `update` tools is judged (`server._SESSION_NEXT`, set by `_session_write()` around the tool bodies); the router, the wake and stall passes, the page-debt call, `client.py post` (a question), the cold call, the terminal-prompt rows, an answer, the recycle write and every `server._update` or `_create_item` call made by server code are not, and a line already stored is read as it was (a line naming no one still reads as the owner's in `steps.is_his`; no data is changed). *N1, refused:* a line of `next` whose first line opens with no `<name>:` (an optional list marker and bold marks, then up to three words and a colon: `steps.opener`) is refused with the line quoted and the fix `open the line with who takes it: <session>:, <owner>: or the outside party's name`; a sub-line (a bullet or number with no name) under a named step belongs to it and is fine, one with no step above it is counted, the words none, clear and `-` that remove lines are not lines, and the ask line a client without the `ask` parameter writes first is lifted before it is judged. *N3, refused:* an update that leaves an open step out of next unmarked (neither done=[n] nor dropped=[n]) and adds a step that is the old text plus words (the old text, its trailing punctuation aside, is a prefix of the new at a word boundary, or the new text equals the old once parentheticals are taken out and it has one the old lacks) is refused: mark the step done or keep it, write the next action as a new line, put the progress on the project page. A genuine rewording that is not a superset passes, and a step marked done is no reword (`next_rules.moves`, `growths`). *N6, refused:* a session's create that leaves no open step (no line of `next` and no step of the owner's that the server would make from the ask, `_model.stood_his`), and an update that removes an item's last open step while it stays open (`done` or `dropped` on the last step, `next=none`, `clear_next=true`) with none of the owner's, with the fix `add the next step … or close the item (complete)`. The update refusal applies only to a write that removes the last open step: an item that already has none is not refused for an unrelated write (held items and older items may legitimately have none), and a close is exempt. *N7, noted, never refused:* `_report_note` (the existing note, extended) names the line of a step that carries a commit id (7 to 40 hex digits holding a digit and a letter), a test count (`12 tests passed`, `all suites green`), a past-tense report (`merged`, `done at`, `live since`, `deployed` or `released` at the start of the line or right after `PR #N`; or a status word in the middle: `is`, `was`, `now`, `already`, `got` or `been` before `live`, `merged`, `deployed` or `released`, or `PR #N is merged`), or a stamp of when work began (`since 09:30`, `at work since`). A status word after once, when, until, after, before, if, unless, wait, check, verify, confirm, whether, that or so is a condition and stays quiet, `release PR #123` and `merge PR #12 after green CI` are actions and are not noted, and a line that opens with the owner's name is exempt. The write goes through and the reply carries the note (the first two lines it found). The text agents learn it from is `_NEXT_WORDS` (the create and update forms, the connector instructions, `tests/tool_text.snapshot.json`). *Part 3:* a label opener (`Status:`, `Note:`, `Result:`, `Done:` and the like, `model_guards.NOT_ACTORS`) is no actor and is refused with that said; a parenthetical role after the name (`my-x (builder):`) passes; `my-x:do` (no space after the colon) is refused with the spacing named (`model_guards.line_hint`); N3 refuses only when the old step leaves the list unmarked, so a step marked `dropped` may be replaced by a longer one; N6 on a held item says a parked item needs the step its wake leads to (`next_rules.NO_STEP_FIX_HELD`), and a hold written with that step passes. *The fill-in* (`next_backfill.py`, `server._migrate_backfill`): a one-off that adds the done steps his past `decision` answers on open items would have left (`<owner>: <the Decide part of the ask> → <his option>`, done by him at the answer's time; an answer saved with no ask reads `answer the ask on the item`; a comment and a note on a step add nothing; an answer already on the checklist is not added again, so a second run adds nothing; a closed item is left as it is). It writes through the migration route (`POST /client/migrate`, a `backfill` write per item, the server recomputing from the item's own answers, `expect` and `expect_sig` checked, author `migration`), only while `[model] migration` is true. *The audit check* (`tools/next_report.py`, `audit.py next-check [--line|--json]`): read only, lists the open items that break N1 to N7 as findings of the category `next rules` (id, project, mover, rule, detail); closed items are left alone. N3 is read only for a step that left `next` unmarked (a marked drop replaced by a longer step is allowed), N4 only for his steps a session ticked since part 1 went live (`next_report.PART1_LIVE`; earlier ticks are history), N6 is not counted again on an item that waits on him (N2 says it), and N2 and N5 read the real `after` links. Suites: `tests/next_backfill_scenario.py`, `tests/next_report_scenario.py`, `tests/next_rules_scenario.py`.
- **The shared PR-state reader** (`pr_state.py`, `server._pr_merged`; `tests/pr_state_scenario.py`). One GET of a pull request per pace window, shared by the three callers that ask whether it is merged: the done check (`pr_merged`), the step proof and the event wait. The first caller to ask reads and the others take its record, so one PR costs one request however many items wait on it; the read is counted once in the GitHub ledger under the first reader's tag. Each caller still keeps the record in its own file. GitHub down or rate-limited backs off (doubling, at most an hour, or what GitHub asked for); a closed PR that was not merged carries `dead`. Locks are one per PR and are never dropped.
- **A step that names its proof is ticked by the server** (`step_proof.py`, `server._proof_item`, `_sweep_proofs`, `_proof_tick`, `client.py sweep-step-proof [--dry-run]`, `tools/backfill_step_proof.py`; `tests/step_proof_scenario.py`, with `tests/released_scenario.py` for the release half). Two events, each one the server can see: `pr_merged` (the pull request is merged: this repository's merges the checkout holds, else GitHub's answer through the shared PR-state reader (`pr_state.py`), the same read the done check uses) and `pr_released` (merged and live: its merge is in the last-good commit release.sh records and in the checkout's HEAD; this repository's pull requests, and those of a repository `[released.repos]` lists, live when their merge is an ancestor of that checkout's HEAD). A step names its proof two ways. *By its words:* `release PR #N`, `release #N`, `release <PR URL>` (also `release PR <PR URL>`), `merge PR #N`, `merge #N`, `merge <PR URL>`, optionally followed by one short parenthetical that names nothing to do (`(in its turn)`) and a closing full stop, with nothing else in the step but its session prefix, is a *pure* step and is ticked. Anything else is a *follow-up*: words after the phrase (`and verify`, `, restart x`, `- check prod`, `then ...`, `(after #M)`), words before it, or a second phrase (`release PR #5 after release PR #4`); the step is not ticked and its mover is told once that the event happened. A condition (`once #N is released`, `when PR #N is merged`, `after #N lands`, and a bare `after #N` on a step that says release, read as released) is never a proof: its mover is told once that it is met. A negated or undecided phrase (`do not yet release`, `cannot release`, `Do NOT, until Friday, release`, `don't merge`, `pre-release`, `decide whether to release`) and a step that only mentions a pull request (`review`, `verify #N is live`, `release notes for #N`) match nothing. A bare `#N` on an item with no anchor in that repository, and no URL, is not matched (it could be another repository's); a step names a `[released.repos]` repository's pull request by its URL (`release PR https://github.com/<owner>/<repo>/pull/<n>`), or by a bare `#N` on an item anchored to that pull request. A short name such as `sibling PR 31` is not read: the parser names a repository only by a URL, and a short name is ambiguous. *By the explicit form:* `step_proof=[{"step": <position or first line>, "proof": "pr_merged <owner>/<repo>#<n>" | "pr_released <owner>/<repo>#<n>" | "none"}]` on `create` and `update` (stored in `metadata.step_proof` under the step's key; the name is `step_proof` because `proof` is already the verifying-state parameter). An explicit proof ticks the step whatever its words say after it; it is refused for a step that is not open, for the owner's own step, and for a `pr_released` of the pull request of a repository that is neither this one nor in `[released.repos]`, and it goes away with the step (done, dropped, reworded, or a close). *What the server does when the event arrives:* it ticks the step (done, by the server, with the event as the reason; History says so); if no open step is left the item closes as a release has always closed one (outcome done, reason naming the pull request); otherwise its mover gets one notice that names the next open step. A pull request closed without a merge ticks nothing: the mover is told once that the step can never be ticked by it, and `get` marks the proof dead in its `step proof:` line. One GitHub cannot be read for ticks nothing and says nothing. Items on hold, waiting on a decision or on a session other than the mover are left alone; a step of another session ticks when its proof arrives; the owner's own step is never ticked by an event. An event seen twice ticks once and tells once (`released_told`). A dry run (`sweep-step-proof --dry-run`) judges each event on its own, so an item with two pending events lists both as ticked where the real pass ticks the first and closes on the second. *The first pass after a deploy:* the server's clock (`_proof_tick`, every 120 s) only logs on its first pass: it writes and tells nothing, logs what it would do and keeps it in `<state>/step-proof-dry.json`, and the passes after it act. `[step_proof] live = true` acts from the first pass, `live = false` never acts (a hand-run `sweep-step-proof` still does). The pass reads git only for the pull requests some open step waits on and keeps the answers by commit, and keeps a GitHub answer that can no longer change (merged, closed unmerged). *The fill-in:* `tools/backfill_step_proof.py` (dry run by default; reads a copy of the store, makes no GitHub call unless `--github`, writes nothing) lists what the server would do for items written before this existed; `--apply` asks the running server to do it.
- **A finished step hands the item to the next session** (`handover.py`, `server._handover`, `_handover_by_hand`, `_gate_step_gap`, `step_proof.proof_gaps`, `audit_checks.check_step_handover`; `tests/step_handover_scenario.py`). A ticked step used to tell only the item's mover, so the session the next step names sat idle until somebody told it. Now, when a step is done (ticked by its proof, or marked `done=[n]` by hand) and the item stays open, the first open step decides: if it opens with another session, the item's mover becomes that session (needs follow, as in a hand-off made by the server) and it is told once, with the step's text. Several open steps are several sessions' turns: only the first is handed over, the later ones when the step before is done. Nothing moves when the next step is the owner's, a front agent's or a retired session's (the front-step rule moves a stuck front step), a name that is no session, or the mover's own; when the session is down or has no pane the item stays with its mover, who is told the step and why, once, and the record says `fallback` (the hand-over runs again only on a later done step; the audit names the session unreached after 30 minutes). An item on hold, waiting on a decision, or whose needs name another session is left alone, as a tick is; a write that names `mover`, `needs` or `agent` makes its own hand-off and this one does not run; a pass whose steps are all other pull requests' pure proofs is quiet. Idempotent by `metadata.handover` = {step hash, kind `moved` or `fallback`, to, at}. The dry run of a sweep does not preview a hand-over. *The write check:* a step whose own leading verb delivers a pull request (`release`, `merge`, `ship`, `deploy`, `land` on a PR number or URL; a step that reviews, verifies, waits for or checks one is no delivery) and has another session's step after it is refused on `create` and `update` when it is new in the write and has no proof (no `step_proof` and not a pure phrase), because without a proof its event ticks nothing and the next session is never handed the item. The refusal is one line: it says to split the step so `merge PR #n` stands alone (it then ticks itself), or, if the PR landing finishes the step, names the exact `step_proof=[{"step": n, "proof": "..."}]` to pass (a release of another repository's URL is given a `pr_merged` proof; for a bare `#n` it says what to pass for another repository's PR). A step that names no pull request is not judged, a script's write is not judged. The History shows the move as the plain `needs:` and `mover: A → B` lines and then `step hand-over: item moved from A to B`; a fallback shows `step hand-over: the next step is X's but that session isn't running; it stays with A` (both read from the item diff, `handover.fresh`). *The audit* (category `step-handover`, in the ten-minute filing pass) reports to the mover what slipped past both: the proof still missing, or the next session never reached after a done step; a front mover gets it through the page key, and it repeats only after a write to the item.
- **The audit's board checks** (`tools/board_report.py`, `audit.py board-check [--line|--json]`; it challenges arbitrary on-hold descriptions and mis-labeled statuses; items on an agent that are not moving are the ten-minute silence pass's): read only, beside `next-check`; lists open items as findings of the category `board checks` (id, project, mover, kind, why), so the audit challenges each item's mover. Two kinds:
- **Archived projects** (`project_archive.py`, `ui/groups.py`, `Board.projects_table` in `ui/render.py`, `server._project_agent_retire` and `server._archive_tick`; `tests/archive_fold_view_scenario.py` and the 6f part of `tests/projects_picker_scenario.py`). The Projects page lists the projects whose page `status:` is one of `[project_scopes] retired_statuses` in a foldable **Archived** group after the active ones, drawn as home's scope subsections are (`groups.fold_block`: a native `<details class="fold sfold" data-fold="projects.archived">`, the heading with the count of archived projects, closed on load; inside it the projects are listed by scope, and the home fold script opens it for a link to a scope that has only archived projects). The project page's group of agents of an archived project says Archived too. **A project is archived when it has no agent and no open item.** The retire that sala already sends (`post project-agent-retire`) takes the agent off the project's agents map in the record and the page's block follows; the call then looks at the project, and when its agents map is empty, no agent is pending for it in the project record and none of the open items (its own, its sub-pages' and the pages that name it as their hub; on hold counts as open) is its, the project is archived: the record's status first (`retired` = who, when, how), then the page's `status:` line follows as a mirror write with a timeline line (the answer carries `archived` and `archive_note`). A page that cannot be written does not fail the retire: it is told in the note, and the record is archived all the same. An archive that cannot be made at all (the record cannot be written) puts the project on the wait list, so the sweep tries again. A project whose record has no `agents` field at all (never staffed) is never archived by a retire (not even by dropping its only pending agent); `tools/archive_empty_projects.py` lists what the rule covers that nothing archived. With open items left the project goes on the wait list `<state>/archive-wait.json` and stays as it is; **the ten-minute archive sweep** (`archive_tick`, last of the ten-minute set) counts the open items of the waiting projects in one read and archives each whose items are gone, whether they were completed or handed to another project; a project that has an agent again, or is archived or gone, leaves the list without a write, and a second sweep over the same state does nothing. A retire answered "not listed" never archives, and a sweep never touches a project that is on no list. Sala sends nothing new: its existing retire call is the trigger.
- **An item's contact** (`contact.py`, `server._contact_*`, `client.py backfill-contact [--apply]`, `tools/backfill_contact.py`; `tests/contact_scenario.py`). The front agent (a user-facing agent) the person the board is for talks to about the item; the item page's side column leads with it as **Owner**, the session name linked to the fleet tool's view of it (`[client] session_view`), the label's hover text "the front agent to talk to about this item", and no row for an item with none. A **Creator** row sits under it (or first, with no contact) on every item, open or closed: whoever created the item, by the name the History's first line gives (`Board.creator_name`), a muted "unknown" when the item names none. Stored as `metadata.contact` (the session's permanent id where the fleet registry resolves it, so a rename changes nothing stored); `get` shows `contact:`, the client JSON rows (`item-facts`, the lists) carry `contact`. *Why not `owner`:* `owner` is a retired term (`tests/retired_terms.toml`; `metadata.owner` is still the stored creator, and the deprecated `owner` parameter of create and update is accepted until `[model] owner_param_until`), so a second meaning would mislead every old reader and session. *Who it is:* an item created by a front agent has that agent; a background agent's create names one of the project's front agents with `contact`, and the server refuses any other name, listing the project's front agents with their mandates and writing nothing; with no name it is the project's `-main` agent (`anchors.fallback_main`). Front and background are told apart by the project page's `agents:` map (`anchors.session_roles`, `contact.fronts`): an entry outside `background:` is front. `update` changes it with the same check, which also accepts the item's creator and its current contact while they are front agents (of any project): the front agent that made the item from talking with the person stays its contact wherever the item is filed, and can be set back to. A completed item keeps the one it had (reopen it first). *When it changes by itself:* the contact retires (the `project-agent-retire` endpoint) or is renamed (`project-agent-rename`): the items it was contact of fall to the project's `-main` agent, or follow the new name, and the endpoint's answer lists them as `contact_moved`; the item moves to another project: the contact stays if it is also a front agent there (a page rename keeps its agents) or is the item's creator (a front agent of any project), else it falls to the new project's `-main`; a stored contact that is no front agent any more reads as the project's `-main` agent until one of these writes it. It is a filing field (`_FILING_ONLY`): setting it moves no state clock, changes no mover, owes no page and sends no notice (a write through `update` still moves `updated`, as any write does; the backfill and the retire, rename and move passes are server writes that leave it). *The fill-in:* `tools/backfill_contact.py` (dry run by default, on a copy of the store: the counts by rule and one line per item that would change; `--json`, `--store DIR`) and `client.py backfill-contact [--apply]` (the running server writes, author `migration`, one filing write per item): an open item gets its creator when that was a front agent (`creator_front`), else its project's `-main` (`main`); a contact that is no front agent any more falls to the `-main` (`retired_contact`); one that has a live contact is left (`has_contact`), as is a project with no `-main` (`no_main`) and a closed item (`closed`). A second run writes nothing. There is no automatic pass at start.
  - `arbitrary-hold`: an item on hold with nothing structural behind it (`render.hold_whys`, the function behind the briefing's "nothing holds it now": an open after item, a pane recycle, needs external, an anchor scoped to it; or a check, a party, a stored wait kind of item, anchor or relay, the owner's recorded words or name in the wait basis) and with next, steps, wait basis and expected reason naming no event (an item, a PR, a link, a wait word with an object, a thing that ends or resets): only a date or vague words.
  - `mislabeled-state`: the stored state differs from `states.derive` (your-step and verifying are stored only), the stored state lacks what it must carry (`states.problems`: your-call with no ask, outside with no external, blocked with no after), or the item is on hold while its words name the owner's decision (should be your-call) or an outside party (should be outside); that reading of the words is skipped for a hold already labeled outside (needs external or the stored state outside) or standing on his recorded words (`cap_ok`). A stored doing item that reads as another state is a mislabeled-state finding only, not also idle.
  The mover of a finding is the item's own (needs, post-wake mover, mover); an item that names none falls to the project's builder, else its `-main` agent, read from the project registry (`router_live.builder`, `anchors.fallback_main`) by `board_report.fallback_mover`, which the CLI installs as `waiting.FALLBACK_MOVER` the way the server does (without the server's last step, the page keeper). A project that names neither leaves the mover empty (printed `-`) and the idle check skipped.
  `--line` prints "Board checks: N item(s) · arbitrary-hold a · mislabeled-state b". Suite: `tests/board_report_scenario.py`.
- **The audit's page agent lines check** (`tools/page_agent_report.py`, `audit.py page-agents [--line|--json]`): read only, beside `board-check`; lists each name on a project page's agent lines that sala no longer knows, and each project page with items and no front agent, as `{page, line, name, to, fix}` (line is `agents line`, `agent line`, `display-name`, `no agents map`, `no front agent`, `other project's session` or `session off the map`; `to` is the agent the daily run tells). `--line` prints "Page agent lines: N line(s) on M page(s)" or "Page agent lines: not checked (the reason)"; `--json` the whole report (`checked`, `note`, `sessions`, `pages`, `findings`); `--sessions FILE` reads a saved copy of `sala sessions --json` instead of calling sala. The rules, the recipient and the run's use of it are under the daily board audit above. Suite: `tests/page_agent_report_scenario.py`.
- **A wait written only in words** (`model_guards.deferral_refs`, `unwaited_deferrals`, the `next_defers` guard; `_tell_close_mentions` and `_next_names_lines` in `server.py`). An item's next deferred on another item in words with no `after`; that item closed and nothing woke the mover, while a front agent kept reporting it as waiting. Three fixes. *Refuse:* a next line that defers on another open item in words (once, after, when, until, till, before, as soon as, then a `board#N` in the same sentence within eight words, a comma between them breaking it unless the id is set off by brackets or commas; and a phrase of `[keywords.model_guards] defer_after_id` right after an id; a negation, a code span, a link target, a fenced block and a server note in parentheses are not read) is refused unless `after` names that item, with the fix `after=["board#N"]` and `ask_by=YYYY-MM-DD` (a wait needs its end), `supplies=` on an update; only the lines the write sets are judged (`defers_text`, like `windows_text`: a stored line is never rejudged, a close is never judged), a closed or unknown item is nothing to wait for, and the other flagged words (when, once, after, until alone) are not refused: the writer's reply and `get` carry a note, nothing is stored, and the filing pass lists them in its flags. *Tell:* on any close (`_release_after`: complete, update with status=completed, a done check, a dropped decision) the mover of every other open item whose next or description names the closed one hears one batched message (the closed item, outcome, reason, the first eight items that named it and a count; through the routing log where the router holds the project, as a wake does); the closer, the closed item and the items that had the closed one in `after` (their wake is their notice) are left out; an item the owner moves goes to the sessions its notices go to. *Show:* `get` (and the reply of a write) lists under `next:` a `named in next:` block, one line for each `board#N` it names (up to eight, itself left out) with the live state: `board#N: closed Sat Mar 28th 09:30 (done)`, `board#N: open, mover X`, `board#N: open, waiting after board#N`, `board#N: not found`. Suite: `tests/item_waits_scenario.py`. *Cleared wait:* the other half of the same gap. A write that clears a wait (`clear_after`, `clear_wait`, `after=none`, or an after list that drops an item) while an open next line still defers on what it cleared in words goes through, and its reply (`_stale_wait_notes` in `server.py`) names each such line, `(note: next line N still waits on board#X, whose wait was just cleared: reword it)`, at most five and then a count, found with the same `deferral_refs` matcher; the lines are the writer's to fix in that turn. Suite: `tests/item_waits_scenario.py`.
- **The tools say mover** (`stackshot_create`, `stackshot_update`, `get`, `brief`, `message`, `whatschanged.py`, step 4b). `owner` is no longer a parameter of create or update: create takes `sender` (the server stores it as the record's creator, still `metadata.owner`, which History shows as "created by") and `mover` / `then` say whose move it is. An `owner` passed in is accepted with a note naming `mover` up to and including `[model] owner_param_until` (read at each call; default `2099-03-03`, so moving it needs no release) and refused after. `get` leads with `mover:` and `then:`; History writes `mover: A → B`; board.md names the mover after the switchover. The notices and replies follow: who acts next is the mover (the notices say "its mover X is a front agent", "…, mover X: <subject>" on a wake line, "then X" for who takes it after the owner); (below) took the old creator role out; a stored key, a config name or a stderr log line keeps `owner`, and "the owner" still means the instance's owner. For 7 days after a release that changes a tool, the brief (the tool and the session-start brief) carries one line, `stackshot changed on <date>: <what>; see docs/design`, generated, not written (*The tool-text gates* below): the server's start compares `tests/tool_text.snapshot.json` with the copy kept at the last start that changed it and appends the difference to `<paths.state>/tool_text.history.json`; the line goes by itself 7 days after its date.
- **The mover holds every power**: an item has a mover and nothing else that acts as an owner. The creator stays stored in `metadata.owner` (the key is kept, no data migration) and is only a History fact, "created by X". An item with no mover is moved by the project's builder (the background agent whose mandate builds), else its -main agent, never by its creator: a start pass (`_mover_backfill_pass`) stores that mover once on every live open item that has none, and the model reads the same default for a new one. A close is told to the mover alone (never to `then` or the creator; the owner sees closes in Landed). A background agent may create items. An item is no longer refiled to its creator's project. The board and the recycle check use mover, needs and `then`, never the stored creator; an item the owner moves sits under its `then` agent, else in a "no agent" group. `then` defaults to the builder doing the work, not the front agent that relayed it: the background mover that hands the item to the owner, else the builder when it is the mover or named in `next` as the one building, else the model's default. The old role word is a retired term (`tests/retired_terms.toml`), so it cannot come back.

### A pane waiting in its terminal

When a session's permission prompt cannot be answered by a hook (a question to the owner, a guarded file, plan mode), the pane waits in its terminal. The fleet tool (sala) tells stackshot,
so the owner has a row in Your move that links the pane, and the row goes when the pane stops waiting:

```
client.py post terminal-prompt open  --session <name|id> --question "<one line>" --sender <label>     # prints the item id, exit 0
client.py post terminal-prompt clear --session <name|id> --sender <label>                             # exit 0, also when nothing is open
```

Exit 1 prints the reason on stderr (a usage line on misuse). Underneath it is one authenticated write, `POST /client/terminal-prompt`, `Authorization: Bearer <machine token>`, body
`{"action": "open" | "clear", "session", "sender", "question"?}` (200 `{item, session, project, repeat}` for open, `{session, cleared: [ids]}` for clear; 400 a check or a refusal; 409 no project with a front agent to file under).

- **One open item per session**, marked `metadata.terminal_prompt` = the session's name as the registry has it. The session is matched through the sala registry (`sessions_registry.py`), so a name, an
  old name (an alias), a sala id and a transcript id reach one item. The item: subject `<session> is waiting for your answer in its terminal` (with the instance's prefix of the project's scope, else `[subject] fallback_prefix`, else its first prefix),
  priority p1, the owner the mover, state `your-step` with the step `Answer <session>'s question in its terminal` (one line, no command), and one next line, `<owner>: open <the pane's address> and answer: `<question>``.
  The address is `[client] session_view` with the name url-quoted, else no link: the pane is named in words ("the pane of <name>"). It is filed under the session's project (the registry row's `project_key`, else its `project`) when that
  project lists a front agent, else under `projects/example-two`; the project's `-main` agent is its creator and takes it after the owner (`then`, as `post question` files), the script is recorded as the creator (`<label> via a script on <machine>`).
- **The question** is one line of at most 200 characters (`--question`; 400 otherwise, and the client refuses it before sending). It stands in next as a code span, because the next guards read prose and the pane's
  words are no wait or step of the item's (a backtick in it becomes an apostrophe); a line over `[metadata.next] line_max` (the address does not count) has the question cut at a word with an ellipsis.
  The guard on alarming words does not judge it (the route sets what `post question` sets).
- **A second `open` for a session** that has an open item changes its next line and files nothing (`repeat: true`, the same id); the same question again writes nothing. After a `clear`, an `open` files a new item.
- **`clear`** closes the session's open item: outcome `done`, reason `the pane stopped waiting`, the end state in `outcome_text`, the description unchanged. None open is a no-op (200, `cleared: []`).
- **The row owes no page and tells no session.** It is a notification that opens and closes with the pane: the writes set `_TERMINAL_ROW` (`server.py`), so no page debt is charged to the script, the mover or the page's keeper, the router
  gets no notice of it, and the close is told to no one (the `_tell_*` notices return nothing). Otherwise it is the normal write path: `_create_item`, then `_update` for the state, and `_update` to close, with every gate.
  The create sets no step, so the state is a second write; if it is refused, the item is closed again as dropped, never left half made.
- A deploy restarts `stackshot.service` (`server.py` changed) and `stackshot-puller.service` (`client.py` changed); a session runs `client.py` fresh at each call, so no recycle is needed for it ([docs/session-reload.md](docs/session-reload.md)).

### A gap in a scheduled job

sala's job-gap watcher (`sala job check`) finds a timer or service of the host that is not in git or has no `X-Model-Lane`. Each gap is one board item, keyed by the unit and the gap's code, moved by the builder of the
repository that holds the job's script, and closed by the watcher itself once the gap is fixed (it used to be one message per gap, with nothing to close):

```
client.py post job-gap open  <timer> <code> --repo <owner/name> [--detail "<text>"] [--sender <label>]   # prints the item id, exit 0
client.py post job-gap clear <timer> <code> [--sender <label>]                                           # prints the closed id; exit 0, also when nothing is open
```

`<code>` is one of `no-description`, `no-lane`, `bad-lane`, `not-git`, `git-differs` (any lower-case word is taken, with its own name in the subject). Exit 1 prints the reason on stderr (a usage line on misuse). Underneath it is one authenticated
write, `POST /client/job-gap`, `Authorization: Bearer <machine token>`, body `{"action": "open" | "clear", "timer", "code", "repo"?, "detail"?, "sender"?}` (200 `{item, timer, code, project, mover, owner, repeat, fallback}` for open,
`{timer, code, cleared: [ids], note}` for clear; 400 a check or a refusal, an unknown repo included, with the known ones named; 409 no project page or no front agent to file under).

- **The repo is mapped to its project page** by `[job_gap.repos]` (`"owner/name" = "<page key>"`; the default, when the table is absent, is none: an instance lists the repos it files job-gap items for). Names are compared as the other repository checks do (case, `[github.moved]`). A repo the table does not list is a 400 that names the ones it does.
- **One open item per (timer, code)**, marked `metadata.job_gap` = `<timer>: <code>`. Subject `<prefix> <timer>: <the gap>` (the scope prefix of the project, else `[subject] fallback_prefix`, else the first prefix; a long unit name is cut so the subject is never refused), p2, filed
  through `_create_item` (every gate applies). The **mover is the project's builder** (`_builder_agent`: the background agent whose mandate builds), who is named in needs and told once, at the create, by the needs notice; the **creator
  is the project's `-main` agent** (a front agent is not woken). `next` is `<builder>: <the fix for the code>` (put the unit file in the repo with an `X-Model-Lane=` line, linked from there), and the description bullets say the gap, the
  fix and who opened it. A project with no builder moves the item by its `-main` agent, and the item, the answer (`fallback: true`) and the client (a line on stderr) say so.
- **No mentions.** A unit name takes an `@` only between letters or digits (`job@1.service`), so it can never start a GitHub mention; an `@` in `--detail` or `--sender` is broken with a zero-width space (`_no_ping`, as the
  other GitHub-bound text), so the mirror pings no one. A code outside the five known ones gets a neutral fix line ("see the watcher's report"), not a guess.
- **A repeat `open` of an open (timer, code)** changes nothing and answers the existing item (`repeat: true`). After a `clear`, an `open` files a new item.
- **`clear`** closes the open item: outcome `done`, a one-line reason within `[close] reason_max`, the end state in `outcome_text`, the description unchanged. None open is a no-op (200, `cleared: []`, a note that says so).
- **The sender** is the script's own label, under the terminal prompt's gate: at most 80 characters on one line and never the owner's name (400); none given is `client.py`. There is no allow-list of labels (the machine token is the
  proof), so no config change is needed for sala's label `unit-gaps`. The item records `<label> via a script on <machine>`.
- **The row owes no page.** It sets `_TERMINAL_ROW` as the terminal prompt does (no page debt for the script, the builder or the keeper, no router notice, a close told to no one) and the guard on alarming words does not judge the
  watcher's text; the builder's notice at the create is the one thing sent. `tool-text`: a client call only, no MCP tool changes.

## The fleet tool

stackshot runs with no fleet tool installed. A fleet tool is what spawns, relaunches and updates the Claude Code panes and
keeps a web view of them; for example sala (howardpark-ai/salamander), a separate tool installed and
documented on its own. The two meet only through [the stackshot–sala contracts](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md), one versioned
document kept on sala's side, and through the config keys that name the other side's paths and commands, all of them
empty by default. With every key unset, what they feed is off and nothing fails: session names are plain text, the `lane`
done check is refused, a change no item covers is left for the next recycle, the injected-text marker is not looked for,
and stackshot runs no fleet command. The points of contact:

- **Session links**: the tool's store of links (`client.session_links`; contract [1, Heartbeat fleet store](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#1-heartbeat-fleet-store)) and its web view
  (`client.session_view`; no section of its own, the link form is in the same one). Its permanent session ids, `session-registry.json`
  in the same directory, are contract [19, Session registry file](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#19-session-registry-file).
- **Lanes**: the tool's lane record (`client.lanes_file`), read by the `lane` done check, and its readings of which lanes
  cannot run (`client.limit_file`, `lane-signin.state` beside the lane record; no longer read by stackshot). A lane word and its old
  alias are one lane in every comparison (the `lane` check; one fixed alias map,
  `lanes.FIXED_ALIASES`, read by `lanes.py`), in either direction, and the current code is shown; no stored data is renamed. stackshot never writes a lane's plan name or display name in its own text, it reads them from the tool's lane-names file
  (`~/.local/state/salamander/lane-names.json`, or the file `STACKSHOT_LANE_NAMES` names; `{"lanes": [{"code", "display", "aliases"}, ...]}`, every lane
  listed), read again when its modification time changes, never by running the tool. A lane word is taken by its code or any alias (a `lane` check is stored by the code); a refusal
  that lists the tool's lanes shows each code with its display name. With the file missing or unreadable, a lane shows as its code and one line goes to
  standard error. Contract
  [12, Lanes and limit state read by stackshot](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#12-lanes-and-limit-state-read-by-stackshot).
- **The injected-text marker** (`said.injected_marker`): what the tool puts first in text it types into a pane. Contract [2, Message marker](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#2-message-marker).
- **Recycles**: the command that asks for one once the box is idle (`fleet.recycle_when_idle`, contract [13, stackshot's release asks for a recycle](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#13-stackshots-release-asks-for-a-recycle)); the end-of-recycle hook,
  `client.py recycled` (its flags, or the environment under `client.recycle_env_prefix`; contract [4, Post-recycle hook](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#4-post-recycle-hook)); the watchdog's reads,
  `client.py recycle-wanted` (prints `<count> <id>[@<epoch>][~<repo>:pr<N>]…`: the second the recycle was asked for, and the item's latest anchored pull request, which the tool waits to see live; [5, Recycle-wanted](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#5-recycle-wanted)), `client.py puller-lag` and the `<state>/clients/puller-lag` file the server rewrites on a change
  ([6, Puller-lag file and path unit](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#6-puller-lag-file-and-path-unit)).
- **Spawns and renames**: `pages.py agent-listed <name>`, which the tool asks before it names a session. Contract [7, Agents-map checks](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#7-agents-map-checks). The New session
  form's calls to `/client/projects` and `/client/project*`: [21, the web terminal's project calls](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md).
- **Reboots**: `reboot-hooks/stackshot-hook` and `reboot-hooks/anchor-hub-hook`, the participants in the tool's reboot. Contract
  [14, Reboot hooks](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#14-reboot-hooks), which points to [reboot-contract.md](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/reboot-contract.md) (version 1, in force).
- **CLI updates**: after the tool updates the CLI it runs its post-update command (sala's `SALA_POST_UPDATE_CMD`; on the host `python3 <checkout>/publish.py bump`). The `bump_versions` tool is a separate way to do the same refresh by hand. Contract [23, CLI update hook](https://github.com/howardpark-ai/salamander/blob/main/docs/stackshot-contracts.md#23-cli-update-hook), described as `publish.py bump`.
- **Naming**: `fleet.tool_name` (prose only) and `fleet.operator_session` (the session the `sessions` tool names for fleet work). No contract.

Every section linked here is in force. The one proposal in that repository, `session-registry-contract-proposal.md`, is adopted only in
part: its two halves that matter here are interfaces 19 and 20 of the same document. Read those, not the proposal.

## Projects: the record first
